- **Supersede.** Within one transaction's chunks, a later op on the same `(tree_id, key)` **supersedes** the earlier one. That happens on a rewrite of the key and on `ROLLBACK TO`. A `Delete{revert}` cancels the key altogether. Replay applies the last op per key (§14). C-T0 v8 I-PROV-WAL keeps every provisional entry equal to its key's last op.
- **No apply at commit.** A large transaction's data becomes readable at its flip, through its provisional entries, and its finalize converts them into ordinary entries. Until then the apply frontier stays below its commit ts (C-T0 v8 §3.5), so a crash replays it from these chunks.

### 12.8 Bulk loads (the rule PLAN-v3 §3.3 asks S-1 to pick)
**S-1 picks: force a checkpoint before the result is published. Page images are not WAL-logged.**
1. **Unlogged writes.** A bulk load (the sorted bulk load of an online index build, C-T6 §7) may write entries **without WAL records**, only into a tree that is **not yet published**: no reader may use it. The tree itself is created by a logged `CreateTree`. Concurrent logged writes into the tree, such as C-T6's write-only maintenance, stay logged as usual.
2. **The forced checkpoint.** The loaded entries become durable only through a checkpoint whose flip follows the load's last unlogged write.
3. **Publication.** The publishing commit (C-T6 write-only → public) may be issued only after that checkpoint's meta is durable (§13 step 5). It carries `RequireCkpt{id}` (§12.3), where `id` is the `ckpt_id` that the forced checkpoint returns once its step 5 is done: never a WAL position or a ts, because a load writes no WAL, so a checkpoint flipped during the load can share the forced checkpoint's `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi` (I-CKPT-DEP). A chunked publishing transaction carries the op in a `Chunk` written after that meta is durable. Catch-up from the WAL (PLAN-v3 §3.5 step 3) is ordinary logged writing.
4. **Crash before publication.** The recovered tree holds only its logged content plus whatever the last image caught of the load. S-3 must treat such a tree as an unfinished build: restart or discard it.

**Pages (I-LINK-ATOMIC).** §13's Exception exempts a load's *entries* from I-CKPT-AT-T, not its pages from I-LINK-ATOMIC (§5.6). Each load step links what it maps, so a discarded build (item 4) can be walked (§6.8) and leaks nothing. Mutant **LOAD-PAGE-SPLIT**: a page mapped one gated step before its parent's pointer. Test `t_load_every_cut` (B-4d, §15 I-LINK-ATOMIC).

Mutant: PUBLISH-BEFORE-META.

**The dependency (I-CKPT-DEP).** A commit that makes visible what only a checkpoint made durable names that checkpoint; in 2.0 only item 3's publication does. Recovery refuses to replay such a commit over an image older than the checkpoint it names (§14 step 3b): that image lacks the load's unlogged entries, and nothing in the WAL restores them, so the replay would publish a tree that silently misses entries (E4's first silent-loss path, `header-design-verify.md` §1.1–§1.3). Its over-refusals are deliberate and fail closed: an open from an image older than the forced one refuses even when the tree was dropped after its publication, or written to after it. Mutants: **DEP-BY-POSITION**: the publication names, and recovery compares, the forced checkpoint's `wal_ckpt_end` or `ts_ckpt_hi` instead of its `ckpt_id`. **DEP-ID-STALE**: the forced checkpoint returns the `ckpt_id` of the meta that was durable before its own step 5. The other mutants and the test are §14 step 3b's.

**Why not page images.** Under (b), a load writes versions at a registered snapshot ts `S`. Such versions may be `≤ T` of a later checkpoint, so a replay filtered by `commit_ts > T` (§14) could never place a logged copy of them. Page images would also carry LPIDs, which are meaningless to logical consumers (N14's replicas and CDC).

**TRUNCATE is not an unlogged load.** PLAN-v3 §3.3 lists TRUNCATE with bulk loads, but nothing in it bypasses the WAL, so it needs no forced checkpoint. It runs as three logged steps:
1. a `CreateTree` of a new, empty tree;
2. the catalog swap (`Ddl`) to the new tree, in the same commit;
3. a `DropTree` of the old tree, run when `W` passes the DDL (§6.8).

**Consequence, owned by D-6.** Unlogged loads are invisible to the WAL stream. Backup and PITR must therefore treat the forced checkpoint's image as a base-image boundary, and replicas must receive that image or rebuild the index (§19 Q5).

---

## 13. Checkpoint (the step order is binding)

At most one checkpoint runs at a time. Its triggers (WAL bytes, dirty volume, time, or a forced checkpoint for a bulk load) are configuration; correctness does not depend on them.

**Step 0 (flip).**
1. Take the epoch gate exclusively. No gated operation is now in flight (§5.6). This generalises PLAN-v3's "pause commit-apply" to every multi-page change.
2. Take `T` := the apply frontier, record `gc_w := gc_w_used` (§10.5) and the `wal_start_lsn` (§12.6) with its `wal_start_prev` and `wal_start_prev_crc` (§2.2). Inside the commit path's I-WAL-TS-ORDER critical section (§12.4), read `wal_ckpt_end` := the append position and `ts_ckpt_hi` := the commit_ts of the last `Commit` or `CommitMarker` appended, or the frontier this boot started from if none was. The two values are read together, in one entry to that critical section, so they describe the same point of the stream. `Chunk` reservations need not be made inside the critical section: a chunk appended concurrently with the read may fall either side of `wal_ckpt_end`, and its provisional entry is dead after recovery (C-T0 v8 §2.4). Every `Commit` and `CommitMarker` reservation is inside it, and that is all the bound below needs. Record the boot id, record the map's root and its level as image `k`'s (§4.1), and set `e := k+1`.

   Every ordinary entry in image `k` was inserted by an apply that started before the flip and after its record was appended, so its record ends at or below `wal_ckpt_end` and its ts is at most `ts_ckpt_hi`. A record appended after the read has no entry in image `k`: its apply inserts are gated operations, and the gate is held exclusively. The unlogged entries of a §12.8 bulk load are not apply inserts. Their ts is the load ts, which is the fence commit's ts `f` (C-T6-rehost §9.1 step 4.2), and `f`'s `Commit` record was appended before the load began, so before the flip: it too ends at or below `wal_ckpt_end`, and `f ≤ ts_ckpt_hi`.
3. Release the gate.

**Step 1 (WAL).** `sync_data` the WAL through `wal_ckpt_end`: the step ends only when `durable_end ≥ wal_ckpt_end` (I-ACK-PREFIX, §12.4), so every byte below it is written and synced, not just the last batch's. This is stricter than PLAN-v3's "through the last record with commit_ts ≤ T", and the reason is out-of-order apply:
- applies run concurrently and finish out of ts order (`e1-mvcc/src/common/commit.rs:35–63`: apply, then `mark_applied` in ts order);
- so the image may hold some entries of a commit `c > T` that started applying before the flip;
- with `synchronous_commit=off`, `c`'s record may not yet be synced;
- syncing through `wal_ckpt_end` guarantees `c`'s record is durable before the meta, so recovery replays `c` wholly (I-CKPT-AT-T, I-REPLAY-IDEM). Without it, a crash could expose part of `c` with no record to complete it (mutant WAL-SYNC-T-ONLY).

Mutant **CKPT-SYNC-OVER-HOLE**: step 1 ends when the latest batch's sync completes while an earlier batch is unwritten. Test `t_ckpt_sync_prefix` (A-7c; the open's verdict A-7d, §15.1): a flip with batch 1 unwritten and batch 2 synced, then a crash after step 5. The test needs no media fault: under the mutant, a class C open fails I-WAL-COVER with XX001.

**The start segment** (I-WAL-START). Before step 4, the segment that holds `wal_start_lsn` exists durably: step 1 has made a record in it durable, or, when `wal_start_lsn` is the append position at a segment's first LSN with nothing written there yet, step 1 first makes that segment's creation, or its recycling rename, durable, as §12.5's writer rule requires before an append. Mutant **START-SEG-NOT-ENSURED**: step 1 does not; a power cut after step 5 then leaves a healthy store whose open finds its start segment missing and fails with XX001.

**Step 2 (image).** The checkpointer writes image `k`, each page to a fresh slot (§4.4). Only step 2 writes the image map, the epoch-`k` map pages. Every write of epoch-`k` content made after the flip outside the map phase, by step 2's data phase, by a claim (§5.6) or by an eviction (§5.2), appends `(page, slot, crc)` to the **image-record list** before it releases the written frame's latch; a page is an LPID, or a map page's position in image `k`'s radix, counted from the leaf level and keyed apart from LPIDs, so that no record for a map page can collide with one for an LPID and a radix level added in epoch `k+1` (§4.1 "Growth") changes no record's meaning. "The image map" in §5.2 and §5.6 means the epoch-`k` map pages with the list applied. The list lives in memory outside the pool, reserved at open (§5.7), under a leaf lock: under it a thread only appends or reads records, with no I/O and no other lock taken. A crash before meta `k` loses the list together with image `k`, which no open uses, and every slot it names was allocated after meta `k−1`, so it is free in image `k−1` (I-FREE-EXACT). Meta `k` is written only after the map phase has applied the list. Step 2 writes, in order:
- every page other than a map page whose epoch-`k` content is not already at a slot the image map references: the dirty frames with content epoch `≤ k`, and the epoch copies (§5.6). Pages that eviction already wrote during epoch `k`, or wrote after the flip as epoch-`k` content (§5.2), are adopted as they are;
- when a written frame was not mutated in epoch `k+1` and its page was not already written for image `k` (the image-record list holds no earlier record for it), its new slot also becomes its current home. The data phase decides both under the frame's latch, and it does not write a page whose epoch-`k` content the list already records: adopting such a write anyway would treat the earlier write's slot as current-only and reusable (§4.4) while image `k`'s map still names it;
- then the **map phase**, once the data phase has ended and no write of epoch-`k` content is in flight, whether a claim (§5.6), a §5.2 previous-epoch victim or a data-phase write: it waits on each such write's latched frame, holding no latch, so the list is complete when it starts, and an append for a page other than a map page after it has started is a debug assertion failure. It writes the epoch-`k` MapLeaf and MapInner pages that changed since image `k−1` or that the list's records name, in post-order (children before their parent). Each is built in a buffer of the checkpointer's own from the page's epoch-`k` content, with the list's records for its entries applied. Its source is decided under that source's latch and re-checked: its epoch copy, if under the copy's latch it is still that page's epoch copy; else its frame, if under the frame's latch its content epoch is `≤ k`; when it is `k+1`, the map phase checks for the epoch copy again, since one may have been installed meanwhile; else the slot the list records for it, which append-before-release guarantees exists once its copy or frame has been written; else its epoch-`k` home. A source is read into the buffer, never loaded into the page table, which names only current frames. The built page is written to a fresh slot, and that slot is recorded in its parent's buffer, up to the root that step 0 recorded (§4.1 "Growth"); a level added in epoch `k+1` is not part of image `k`. The map phase holds at most one latch at a time. A map page whose epoch-`k` content it has taken counts as written for image `k` (§5.6 "When it copies"). It takes a map page's epoch copy under the copy's latch and, still under that latch, detaches it, so that the frame is no longer that page's epoch copy and no claim can evict it; it returns the frame to the `R_EPOCH` reserve only after it has released that latch and before it latches its next source, so it never holds the latch of a frame in the reserve (§5.6 "Reserve") and at most one detached frame is ever out of the reserve, inside step 2's share of §5.6's Bound. A claim that wins the frame's latch afterwards finds it no longer the copy it chose and moves on, so no record for a map page the map phase has built is appended later, and such an append is a debug assertion failure. A slot the list records for a map page it rewrites leaves §4.4 item 2 once the new one is written; it is reusable only if no other reference of §4.4 holds it, since it may be the page's current home. The map phase never makes a slot a map page's current home: only a write of the page's live frame does, as §4.1 says. Image `k`'s slot bitmap is derived from its built map, not from the list (I-FREE-EXACT);
- then the free set **last**: allocate the slots for the FreeSet pages first (growing the file first if needed), then serialise both bitmaps of image `k`, with those slots marked used (I-FREE-EXACT).
  - **Sizing.** The bitmap size depends only on `slot_count` and `next_lpid`. If allocating the FreeSet slots grows the file, `slot_count` changes, so the checkpointer recomputes the number of FreeSet pages after the growth and allocates the difference.
  - **Convergence: the condition.** Say the original allocation needs `n` FreeSet pages but finds only `f < n` free slots.
    - The growth adds `g ≥ 16` slots (§4.5: 1 MiB at the largest page size).
    - The rest of the original allocation, `r = n − f` pages, lands in those new slots.
    - Covering the `g` new slots needs `extra` more pages: at most `⌈g / (8·(page_size − 24))⌉` FreeSetLeaf pages plus one page per radix level. These must land in the new slots too.

    One growth is therefore enough exactly when **`r + extra ≤ g`**. `extra < g` alone is not enough.
  - **Convergence: why it holds.** `r ≤ n`, the whole FreeSet:
    - while the file is at most 16 MiB, `g` is at its 1 MiB floor and the FreeSet is a handful of pages;
    - above that, `g = file_len / 16` slots, while the FreeSet is about `(slot_count + next_lpid) / (8·(page_size − 24))` pages, under 1/30,000 of that sum even at 4 KiB, the smallest page size.

    So `n + extra ≤ g` unless `next_lpid` exceeds `slot_count` by orders of magnitude.
  - **Convergence: the check.** The checkpointer still checks `r + extra ≤ g` after the growth. If the check fails, it grows again and repeats the check. Each further growth places at least `g − extra > 0` of the outstanding pages, so the repetition ends.
  - Growth does not change `next_lpid`, because FreeSet pages are physically addressed.

Step 2's data phase ends only when no epoch-`k` content is outstanding: it waits on a latched frame (§5.6's claim and install) (mutant STEP2-NO-WAIT, §15). It waits holding no latch, map latches included. It records a data write in the image-record list, not in a map page; it latches a current MapLeaf only after the written frame's latch, to set a current home; and in the map phase it holds at most one latch at a time (§5.3) (mutant CKPT-WAIT-HOLDING-MAP, §15).

Pages created or mutated only after the flip are not part of the image.

**Step 3.** `sync_all` the page file.

**Step 4.** Write meta `k` (the file's static fields, `ckpt_id = k`, `T`, `gc_w`, epoch, the roots, `map_level`, `next_lpid`, `slot_count`, `wal_start_lsn`, `wal_start_prev`, `wal_start_prev_crc`, `wal_ckpt_end`, `ts_ckpt_hi`, `boot_id`, CRC) twice: copy 0 to slot `1 + (k mod 2)` and copy 1 to that parity's replica block (§2.2, §2.4), in either order, both before step 5 (I-META-COPIES; mutants REPLICA-SKIP and COPY-INDEX-OFF, §2.2). Checked at meta write: `T ≤ ts_ckpt_hi` and `wal_start_lsn ≤ wal_ckpt_end ≤ durable_end`. A violation is fail-stop (§9.2), as for `gc_w ≤ T` (§10.5).

**Step 5.** `sync_all`. Meta `k` is now the durable image.

**Step 6.** Only now, one generation behind (I-TWO-GEN, §4.4):
- move to free the slots pending since the previous step 6: those that image `k−2` referenced and neither image `k−1` nor image `k` references (after an open, the open's pending list: those the previous image referenced and the opened image does not, §14 step 2);
- make pending the slots that image `k−1` references and image `k` does not;
- recycle the WAL segments wholly below meta `k−1`'s `wal_start_lsn` (after an open, the opened meta's), lowest first, each rename durable before the next (§12.1, I-WAL-START). Mutant **RECYCLE-UNORDERED**: a rename is issued before the previous one is durable, or out of order, so a power cut can leave a higher segment recycled and a lower one present.

**I-META-ATOMIC.** A recovered meta never points at an unwritten or unsynced page: step 3 precedes step 4. Mutant: META-BEFORE-SYNC.

**I-CKPT-AT-T.** Image `k` is a consistent cut: every gated operation is wholly before or wholly after the flip. In image `k`:
- **(a)** every commit with ts `≤ T` is wholly present, up to GC `≤ gc_w`. For every snapshot `S` with `gc_w ≤ S ≤ T`, reads on the image equal reads on the committed state at `T`;
- **(b)** every ordinary entry with ts `> T` belongs to a commit `c` with `T < c ≤ ts_ckpt_hi` whose records end at or below `wal_ckpt_end` and are durable when meta `k` is written (step 1). Recovery replays `c` wholly **if those records still read back**; if they do not, I-WAL-COVER refuses to open the image as it is (§14 step 3a). An unlogged bulk-load entry (the Exception below) with ts `f > T` has its fence commit `f`'s record below `wal_ckpt_end` in the same way, and a recovered frontier below `f` fails the same check. No open therefore holds an entry above its recovered frontier;
- **(c)** every ordinary entry belongs to a committed transaction (N2). An image may hold **provisional entries** (§7.3 tags 6–8) of any transaction, committed or not. After recovery every one of them is dead (C-T0 v8 §2.4: its `txn.boot` is not the new boot id), so none is ever read as data. A committed transaction's data is in the image as ordinary entries if its commit ts is `≤ T` (`T` is the apply frontier, C-T0 v8's `M`), and otherwise it is replayed from its durable chunks (§12.6, §14). Mutant CKPT-T-VISIBLE (`T` taken as `visible_ts`).

**Exception.** The unlogged entries of a §12.8 bulk load in a tree that is **not yet published** are exempt from (a)–(c):
- they come from no commit;
- an image may hold any part of them;
- no reader can use the tree until after a forced checkpoint makes them whole (I-BULK-PUBLISH).

(a)'s read equality is over published trees.

Mutants: FLIP-NO-GATE (a split or pack straddles the flip), WAL-SYNC-T-ONLY.

**I-FREE-SAFE** and **I-TWO-GEN** (§4.4). Mutants: FREE-EARLY (step 6's frees moved before step 5), FREE-ONE-GEN and RECYCLE-ONE-GEN (§4.4).

---

## 14. Recovery

1. Read page 0 and the four meta places, and select the newest meta `k` and the previous meta by §2.2's open rule (I-META-COPIES, I-META-SLOT). A meta whose magic and CRC are valid but whose `T > ts_ckpt_hi` or `wal_start_lsn > wal_ckpt_end` is corrupt: the open fails with XX001, with no fallback (§2.2). Mutant **META-RANGE-SKIP**: this open-time check is removed (test `t_cover_ts_cross_check`, §15 I-WAL-COVER).
2. Load the map root, at `map_level` (§4.1), and the free set of image `k`. Every slot not referenced by image `k` is free, including the slots eviction wrote after meta `k`, except those the previous image references (I-TWO-GEN, §4.4): they stay protected until the first step 6 after the open, and those of them image `k` does not reference are the open's pending list (§13 step 6). Then build the root table from image `k`'s directory (§6.8), before replay.
3. **Pass 1:** if the segment that holds `wal_start_lsn` is missing, the open has detected damage (I-WAL-START, §12.5): a read-write open fails with **XX001** (detail: start segment missing, with `wal_start_lsn`) before the walk, and writes nothing; a read-only open serves salvage with the valid end at `wal_start_lsn` (§14.1). Otherwise, walk the WAL from `wal_start_lsn` to the valid end (§12.5), collecting the set of `(txn, commit_ts)` of every `CommitMarker` with `commit_ts > T`. It checks each record for a splice as well (§12.5, I-WAL-CHAIN), and ends at the first one.

3a. **Coverage check (I-WAL-COVER).** Pass 1 also records the largest `commit_ts` of the `Commit` and `CommitMarker` records it reads. The WAL **covers** image `k` iff:
   - the valid end (§12.5: the LSN at which the walk stopped, never an end-of-file) is at or above meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end`; and
   - `max(T, that largest commit_ts) ≥ ts_ckpt_hi`.

   If it does not, there is **WAL loss**: bytes that step 1 of checkpoint `k` synced before meta `k` was written no longer read back, and image `k` may hold entries of the commits they recorded (I-CKPT-AT-T (b)). The open then fails with **XX001** (detail: WAL loss, with the valid end, `wal_ckpt_end`, `T` and `ts_ckpt_hi`) **before pass 2, and writes nothing**: no replay mutation, no eviction write and no truncation (step 5). The files stay exactly as found, including any intact records behind the damage. A read-only open serves the salvage state instead (§14.1).
   - *Why it is sound.* Suppose image `k` held an entry with ts `c` above the recovered frontier.
     - An ordinary entry's commit record lies below `wal_ckpt_end` (§13 step 0) and at or above `wal_start_lsn` (§12.6, I-WAL-TS-ORDER), and it was not replayed. So the walk stopped below it, and the first condition fails.
     - An unlogged bulk-load entry (§12.8) has ts `c = f`, its fence commit's ts. `f`'s `Commit` record was appended before the load, so before the flip, and so it ends at or below `wal_ckpt_end`. Since `f >` the frontier `≥ T`, the record lies at or above `wal_start_lsn` (§12.6), and it was not replayed. The first condition fails the same way.

     So no store opens holding an entry above its recovered frontier, and C-T0 v8's sequencer restart at frontier + 1 (C-T0 v8 §3.7) never reissues the ts of an entry in the trees (I-ENTRY-UNIQUE). The check has no false negatives for that hazard, but it is not exact (below). The second condition checks the same floor directly from the meta. In a correct store it never fails alone; it turns a breach of §12.4's "apply waits for the append" or of I-WAL-TS-ORDER into an XX001 here instead of a reissued ts.
   - *What it also refuses.* It fails on any loss of bytes below `wal_ckpt_end`, whether or not the image depends on them:
     - records of commits that were appended but not yet applied at the flip, which are synced and may be acked;
     - chunks of a chunked transaction that committed after the flip, whose chunks lie below `wal_ckpt_end` and whose marker lies above it;
     - bytes that held **no committed data at all**: chunks of a transaction that never committed, a `SegmentEnd`, or records `≤ T` with nothing above `T` behind them.

     This is deliberate. The check compares positions, so once the bytes are gone it cannot tell what they held, and detected damage below `wal_ckpt_end` is never silent.
   - *Why never open the image as it is.* The image would serve part of a commit whose record is gone, above the frontier, so the state would be neither atomic nor a ts-prefix, and the sequencer would then reissue that commit's ts. E4 found this in 451 class MW rounds (X-5c-F3, MW-ORPHAN).
   - Mutants:
     - **COVER-CHECK-SKIP**: the check is removed;
     - **COVER-END-AT-T**: `wal_ckpt_end` is taken as the end of the last record with commit_ts `≤ T`, WAL-SYNC-T-ONLY's position;
     - **COVER-AFTER-TRUNCATE**: the check runs after step 5 or pass 2;
     - **TS-HI-CHECK-SKIP**: the second condition is removed;
     - **COVER-AT-EOF**: the first condition compares against a short segment's end-of-file (§12.5).

     Tests: `t_wal_loss_below_ckpt_end` and `t_cover_ts_cross_check` (A-7d, the bulk-load variant's load also B-4d; §15 I-WAL-COVER, §15.1).

   **A splice** (§12.5, I-WAL-CHAIN) found by pass 1 fails a read-write open in the same way, whether or not the coverage check passes. The open fails with **XX001** (detail: WAL splice, with the record's LSN, its `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`, and the walk's predecessor's start and `crc`, plus the coverage result) before pass 2, and writes nothing. That holds wherever the splice lies, at or above `wal_ckpt_end` included. A read-only open serves salvage instead (§14.1), with the valid end cut back to the **splice cut**. The cut is the start of the first accepted record that ends after `m = min(the walk's predecessor's start, prev_lsn)`, or the walk's start if no accepted record ends after `m`. When `prev_lsn` equals the walk's predecessor's start and only `prev_crc` differs, `m` is that start, and the cut is the predecessor itself. Every record from that point on is suspect, because one of the two histories was overwritten there. If the splice record is genuine, the walk's history differs from the record covering `prev_lsn` on. If it is foreign, it differs from the splice on. The cut keeps neither.
3b. **Dependencies (I-CKPT-DEP).** After step 3a and before pass 2, recovery reads every `RequireCkpt{id}` op that pass 2 would apply: in a `Commit` with `commit_ts > T`, and in a `Chunk` of a transaction in pass 1's set. Every op is checked, not only the first. If any `id` exceeds the opened meta's `ckpt_id`, the image lacks entries a replayed commit makes visible (§12.8): a read-write open fails with **XX001** (detail: checkpoint dependency, with the record's LSN, the `id` and the opened `ckpt_id`) before pass 2, and writes nothing. A read-only open serves salvage at the **dependency cut**: the start of the commit record (the `Commit`, or a chunked transaction's `CommitMarker`) of the first transaction, in LSN order, whose op fails. Pass 2 then applies only the `Commit` records below the cut and the `Chunk` records of transactions whose `CommitMarker` lies below it (§14.1).
   - Mutants: **DEP-CHECK-SKIP**: the check is removed. **DEP-FIRST-ONLY**: the check stops after the first `RequireCkpt` it reads. **DEP-CHECK-LATE**: the check runs after pass 2 or after step 5. **DEP-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT**: a read-only open replays the failing transaction, or a record at or after the cut. DEP-BY-POSITION and DEP-ID-STALE are §12.8's.
   - Test `t_ckpt_dep` (A-6a, A-7d, B-4d; §15.1). At A-6a, `RequireCkpt` encodes and decodes inside `Commit` and `Chunk` records, the fuzz targets reach it, and no other op's bytes decode as it. At A-7d, on S3 for the load and its publication (the test makes the unlogged inserts, forces the checkpoint, and issues the publishing commit with the id the checkpoint returned): a checkpoint, a load, its forced checkpoint `k`, its publication and later acked commits, then a post-sync LOST on both copies of meta `k` and a crash. The read-write open fails with XX001 before pass 2 and leaves every file byte-identical; the read-only open serves the state cut before the publication, with every acked commit below the cut and the tree unpublished. Variants: (a) TORN, and MISDIRECTED to a page slot, instead of LOST; (b) two loads, publication A depending on `k` and publication B on `k+1`, with both copies of meta `k+1` lost: the open refuses on B; (c) a checkpoint flipped during the load, after the fence commit, so that it has the forced checkpoint's `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`, with the forced meta lost: refused; (d) the tree dropped after its publication, and logged writes into it after its publication: each refused; (e) a chunked publication: refused; (f) faultless, a crash at every step from the load to the commits after the publication: every open succeeds, and the tree holds every loaded entry. DEP-CHECK-SKIP fails the main case: the open succeeds, and a scan through the published tree misses the loaded entries. DEP-FIRST-ONLY fails (b). DEP-BY-POSITION fails (c): it opens the image of the checkpoint flipped during the load, which lacks the load's later entries. DEP-CHECK-LATE fails the main case's byte-identity: a pass-2 eviction or step 5's truncation has written. DEP-ID-STALE fails the main case: the publication names meta `k−1`, and the open from it replays the publication. DEP-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT fails the read-only check: the tree is served published and short. At B-4d the main case runs with B-4d's load and C-T6's publication.
4. **Pass 2:** walk the same span in LSN order, and apply:
   - every `Commit` with `commit_ts > T`;
   - every `Chunk` of a transaction in pass 1's set, at that set's `commit_ts`.

   A chunk whose transaction has no marker is ignored. Records with `commit_ts ≤ T` are skipped: they are in the image.

   **DropTree.** A replayed `DropTree` sets `dropping` and does nothing else (nothing if the id is absent). Its walk runs at step 6. Pass 2 runs no walk. Mutant **REPLAY-DROP-WALK**: pass 2 walks, so a read-only open frees a page (§14.1; test `t_ro_open` with a replayed `DropTree`, A-7d). Its state change includes the root-table entry's, and a replayed `CreateTree` installs its entry, as in normal operation (§6.8).

   **Chunk supersede (I-CHUNK-SUPERSEDE).** For a chunked transaction, each op inserts or replaces the entry `key@commit_ts`, and a `Delete{revert}` deletes it if present. A replace or delete frees the replaced value's overflow chain in the same gated operation (§6.7). So the last op per key wins, whatever the image already holds for that key at that ts. An op that inserts an entry identical to the present one is a no-op. Ops of a `Commit` record keep I-ENTRY-UNIQUE's XX001 for a different value. Mutant SUPERSEDE-AS-CONFLICT: a superseding op is treated as a conflict (XX001).
4a. **Fall-back (I-FALLBACK).** The newest image **fails at open** when an integrity check (§3.3) fails on a page read through image `k`'s references, in step 2 or in pass 2. Nothing else falls back: a refusal in step 1, 3, 3a or 3b, a read error (58030), and a failure in steps 5–8 are handled as those steps and §9.2 say. Image `k` can fail in pass 2 after pass 2 has evicted: those evictions wrote only slots that image `k` does not reference, and none that image `k−1` references when step 2 read its free set (§4.4, I-TWO-GEN), and an outcome of this step that writes nothing writes nothing more than they did. The open then:
   1. Falls back only to the previous image (§1): meta `k−1`, and only when step 2 read meta `k−1`'s free set and so protected its slots (I-TWO-GEN). With no previous meta, or with its free set failing a read in step 2, there is nothing to fall back to, wherever image `k` failed, and the open fails with **XX001** (detail: image `k` failed, with no previous image). Image `k−1` is as it was when meta `k` is the newest meta that was synced and every open since meta `k` was synced read meta `k−1`'s free set too (I-TWO-GEN). Otherwise a slot of image `k−1` that a later write reused fails I-INTEGRITY when it is read (§3.3): at open, as item 2's integrity failure, or at a later read (§9.2); never silently (§2.2, "One fall-back, and only one").
   2. Discards everything it built from image `k`, and runs step 1's range check on meta `k−1` and steps 2–3b from meta `k−1`: the load of image `k−1`, pass 1 from its `wal_start_lsn`, and its coverage, splice and dependency checks, each against meta `k−1`. A refusal there is handled as if meta `k−1` were the newest meta, with the read-only salvage §14.1 gives it. An integrity failure in image `k−1` is XX001 in both opens: no open falls back two generations. Pass-2 evictions made from image `k` before it failed wrote only slots that neither image `k` nor image `k−1` references (§4.4), so image `k−1` is as item 1 says. An integrity failure in image `k−1` in pass 2, after the retire write (below), leaves meta `k` retired: the next open selects meta `k−1` and refuses as this one did.
   3. **The proof.** The fall-back is **proven** when, beyond item 2, pass 1 from meta `k−1` reaches meta `k`: its valid end is at or above meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end`, and the largest `commit_ts` it read, or meta `k−1`'s `T` if larger, is at or above meta `k`'s `ts_ckpt_hi`. Then every commit whose entries image `k` holds is replayed from the WAL, and every unlogged entry that image `k` holds and image `k−1` lacks belongs to a load whose publication names checkpoint `k` or later, which step 3b refuses, or whose tree is still unpublished (§12.8). So the open recovers the committed state meta `k` would have given; only GC differs, and an older image holds more versions, not fewer.
   4. **Proven:** a read-write open retires meta `k` (below), then runs pass 2 and steps 5–8 from meta `k−1`. It reports the fall-back: image `k`'s failure, and both metas' `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`. A read-only open serves the same state and writes nothing.
   5. **Unproven:** a read-write open fails with **XX001** (detail: image `k` failed and the fall-back is unproven, with the valid end and the largest `commit_ts` against meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`), and writes nothing more (above): no meta place, no WAL byte and no slot that image `k` or image `k−1` references changes. A read-only open serves meta `k−1`'s recovered state as salvage, at its frontier, and reports the same values (§14.1). Only an explicit rollback opens it read-write.

   **Retiring meta `k`.** Before a read-write fall-back writes anything from meta `k−1` (a pass-2 eviction, step 5's truncation, the boot checkpoint), it retires meta `k` in two writes: first it overwrites with zeros every place of the other parity that holds a copy of meta `k` (a misplaced copy, §2.2) and runs `sync_all`; then it overwrites both places of meta `k`'s own parity with zeros and runs `sync_all`. Only the pass-2 evictions made from image `k` before it failed can precede the retire write (above). No place then holds a copy of meta `k`, so no later open selects it and opens image `k` over slots this open reuses, and no later open fails on it as a misplaced copy newer than every valid one (I-META-SLOT); the next open selects meta `k−1` as the newest, and the boot checkpoint later writes `ckpt_id` `k` to its parity's places, as any checkpoint does. The misplaced copies go first: zeroing meta `k`'s own places while a misplaced copy survives would leave the next open XX001. A crash before the second `sync_all` completes leaves either a copy of meta `k` in a place of its own parity, with image `k` as it was, so that the next open decides again, or meta `k` retired. If a retire write fails, the open fails with its error and writes nothing more. A read-only open and a refused open retire nothing.

   **Explicit rollback** (PLAN-v3 §10 item 16). A read-write open may be asked to roll back one generation, naming the `ckpt_id` it abandons. It proceeds only when the newest meta has that `ckpt_id` and its image fails at open; otherwise it fails with **55000** and writes nothing. It then falls back as a proven fall-back does, with items 1 and 2 against meta `k−1`, waiving only the proof (item 3). It reports what it abandons: meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`, against the valid end and the frontier it recovered. Commits whose records lie past that valid end are lost; step 5 truncates there. It does not apply to WAL loss or a splice under a newest image that opens: there a read-write open stays refused (PLAN-v3 §10 item 8), and the remedy is the read-only salvage and an export (§14.1).

   Mutants:
   - **FALLBACK-OFF**: an image that fails at open is XX001 with no fall-back, as at draft 7.10.
   - **FALLBACK-NO-PROOF**: the fall-back skips the proof (item 3).
   - **FALLBACK-TWO-GEN**: the fall-back takes the other parity's candidate whatever its `ckpt_id`.
   - **RETIRE-SKIP**: a read-write fall-back writes from meta `k−1` before it retires meta `k`, or never retires it.
   - **RETIRE-ONE-COPY**: the retire write zeroes the primary copy only.
   - **ROLLBACK-SKIPS-PREV-CHECKS**: an explicit rollback skips meta `k−1`'s own checks (item 2).

   Test `t_fallback` (A-7d; §15.1):
   - *Proven:* a post-sync fault (LOST or TORN) on a page of image `k` that the open reads (a MapLeaf, a FreeSet page, a directory page, and a leaf pass 2 replays into, each a case), with acked commits after meta `k`. The read-write open falls back, recovers every acked commit and reports the fall-back, and every later image passes amcheck; a hook asserts that every place that held a copy of meta `k` is zeros, and synced, before the open's first write from meta `k−1`, and that every write before the retire went to a slot that neither image `k` nor image `k−1` references. The read-only open serves the same state and leaves every file byte-identical. FALLBACK-OFF fails it: the open is refused. RO-WRITES fails the read-only form: it retires meta `k`.
   - *Unproven:* as proven, plus a post-sync LOST on a synced WAL record, holding an acked commit, between meta `k−1`'s and meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end`. The read-write open fails with XX001, and every meta place, every WAL file and every slot that image `k` or image `k−1` references is byte-identical afterwards; where image `k` fails before pass 2, every file is. The read-only open serves the state at its walk's frontier, never a version of the lost commit, and reports the shortfall. An explicit rollback naming meta `k` opens read-write at that frontier, and the next open selects the boot checkpoint's meta. FALLBACK-NO-PROOF fails it: the plain read-write open succeeds without the lost commit. An explicit rollback whose meta `k−1` fails its own coverage check (a second LOST, below meta `k−1`'s `wal_ckpt_end`) fails with XX001; ROLLBACK-SKIPS-PREV-CHECKS fails it, opening with an entry above its frontier that a read at that entry's ts sees. An explicit rollback asked of a store whose newest image opens fails with 55000.
   - *Two generations:* the other parity's candidate is meta `k−3`, both copies of meta `k−1` having been lost: the open fails with XX001. FALLBACK-TWO-GEN fails it: it selects meta `k−3`, which a hook sees as a read through meta `k−3`'s references, and its XX001 detail is not "with no previous image".
   - *Retire:* a hook corrupts the bytes of one read of a page of image `k` at the first open only, so that the page reads correctly afterwards. The read-write open falls back with proof and retires meta `k`, and a crash follows a pass-2 eviction into a slot that image `k` references (a hook places it). The next open selects meta `k−1` as the newest, recovers every acked commit, and every image passes amcheck. RETIRE-SKIP fails it: the next open selects meta `k`, whose image now reads, and meets the overwritten slot (XX001, or amcheck fails). RETIRE-ONE-COPY fails it the same way, through meta `k`'s replica. A crash between the two zeroing writes: the next open decides again and recovers the same state. *Misplaced variant:* one copy of meta `k` was misdirected into a place of the other parity before the fault (meta `k−1` keeps its other copy), and a crash follows the retire write: the next open selects meta `k−1` and recovers every acked commit; a retire that zeroes only meta `k`'s own places gives XX001 (I-META-SLOT). *Unread variant:* a hook fails one read of meta `k−1`'s free set in step 2 of the first open only: that open fails with XX001 (no previous image) and writes nothing, and the next open falls back with proof.
5. Truncate the WAL at the valid end.
6. Resume the walks of every `dropping` directory entry (§6.8), as a B-6 boot job.
7. Optionally run `amcheck` (A-8).
8. Run the **boot checkpoint** (§13). It runs every step 0–6 and **always writes a new meta** (`ckpt_id` one above the recovered meta's), even when no page is dirty. No transaction starts until its step 5 has made that meta durable. C-T0 v8's **boot id** (C-T0 v8 §2.5) comes from it, and is not the checkpoint epoch `e` (one higher after the flip). Its boot id is the newest, in serial-number order (§12.5), of the low 32 bits of its `ckpt_id`, one above the opened meta's `boot_id`, and one above the newest boot id of any record the recovery walk accepted. After a meta rollback (§2.2, X5-R5) the walk can cross into a boot whose meta was lost, and whose `ckpt_id` this boot checkpoint would otherwise repeat. A boot whose id was raised that way can be followed by one whose `ckpt_id` is below that id, which the opened meta's `boot_id` bounds. Meta `k+1`'s `boot_id` records it. Mutant **BOOT-ID-CKPT-ONLY**: the boot id is the low 32 bits of `ckpt_id` regardless. Test `t_boot_id_after_rollback` (A-7d, class MP): two successive rollbacks to meta `k`, across boots `b` and `b+1`, each made by a post-sync LOST on both copies of the boot meta `k+1`; boot `b` leaves an unmarked chunked TxnId `(id, 1)`, and boot `b+1` commits a chunked transaction. Recovery from `k` must never attach `b`'s chunks to `b+1`'s marker; BOOT-ID-CKPT-ONLY does. Mutant **BOOT-ID-NO-META-FLOOR**: the opened meta's `boot_id` term is omitted. Variant of `t_boot_id_after_rollback` (A-7d, class MP), *raised id, then no records*, with `t_meta_rollback_crosses_boot`'s fixture constraints (§12.5): boot `b−1` writes meta `k` to its parity's places and crashes; boot `b` recovers from `k` (id `k+1`), writes meta `k+1` to the other parity's places, appends and acks, and both copies' writes are LOST before a crash; boot `b+1` recovers from `k`, its walk crosses `b`'s records (id `k+2`), and both copies of its meta `k+1` are LOST again before a crash; boot `b+2` recovers from `k`, its walk crosses `b`'s and `b+1`'s records (id `k+3`), its meta `k+1` (`boot_id` `k+3`, `wal_start_lsn` the append position `p`) is durable, and it crashes before any record of its own reads back; boot `b+3` recovers from meta `k+1`, its walk from `p` accepts nothing, it writes meta `k+2`, appends and acks `synchronous_commit=on` commits from `p`, and crashes before its next checkpoint; a LOST write on both copies of meta `k+2` leaves its places holding meta `k`. The open selects meta `k+1` and must replay boot `b+3`'s acked commits. BOOT-ID-NO-META-FLOOR gives boot `b+3` the id `k+2`, older than meta `k+1`'s `boot_id`, so the walk stops at `p`, passes I-WAL-COVER and returns `Ok` without them. Chunks of the previous boot that have no marker belong to transactions that ended without one (aborted, cut off by the crash, or committed with no ts), all ended before this flip, so they do not hold back `wal_start_lsn` (§12.6). A **read-only open** (N11) skips this step and has no boot id; every provisional entry is dead to it (§14.1).

Afterwards, the apply frontier is the largest replayed `commit_ts`, or `T` if nothing was replayed. By I-WAL-COVER it is at least `ts_ckpt_hi`, so it is at least the ts of every entry in the trees, bulk-load entries included. C-T0 v8 restarts its sequencer and watermark from that value and from `gc_w` (C-T0 v8 §3.7). Provisional entries found in the image are dead and are ignored by every read (§8).

**Post-sync damage.** I-REDO-COMPLETE assumes the device keeps what a completed sync covered. When it does not, recovery's outcome depends on where the damaged bytes lie:
- **Below `wal_start_lsn`:** never read; the image holds what they recorded.
- **In `[wal_start_lsn, wal_ckpt_end)`:** detected by I-WAL-COVER (step 3a).
- **At or above `wal_ckpt_end`:** the valid end stops at the damage. The state is the ts-prefix up to it (I-WAL-TS-ORDER), and no image entry depends on what is lost. But synced, possibly acked, commits past it are lost **without detection**, unless the damaged bytes still pass every stop check and an intact record behind them names a different predecessor (a splice, below), and step 5 truncates any intact records behind it. A single-copy WAL keeps no durable record of how far it was synced past the last meta (§19 T4).
- **Bytes that still pass every stop check but are not what was appended** (a splice), anywhere in the walked span: detected by I-WAL-CHAIN when an intact later record names a different predecessor (§12.5). A read-write open fails with XX001, and a read-only open serves salvage cut before it (step 3a). Not detected when no record follows (a lost tail), or when the predecessor the intact record names is byte-identical, at the same position, to the one the walk accepted, or their CRCs collide (§12.5).
- **A meta copy:** one copy lost, torn or misdirected after its sync, or unreadable: the other copy opens the same meta (I-META-COPIES). **Both copies of the newest meta** (a device that dropped the sync that covered them): the open selects the previous meta, whose image and WAL span are intact (I-TWO-GEN), and its walk crosses into the newer records (§12.5), so what is lost is only what the dropped sync also lost of the WAL, as above (§19 T4). **Both copies of the two newest metas:** the open selects an older meta; a reused slot of its image fails I-INTEGRITY when read (§3.3), a recycled start segment fails I-WAL-START, and a publication over it fails I-CKPT-DEP. An I-INTEGRITY failure at open there can make step 4a fall back once from the selected meta, to an image also outside the two generations, whose proof holds against the selected meta only: a reused slot of it fails I-INTEGRITY at open, or at a later read (§9.2). None is silent, but such an open can succeed and then fail reads.
- **A page of the newest image:** the open falls back to the previous image when the log proves complete, and refuses otherwise (step 4a, I-FALLBACK). A page that only a later step reads (a resumed drop walk, amcheck, the boot checkpoint) fails as that step and §9.2 say.
- **An older boot's record left at a valid position:** stopped by the ordered boot-id check (§12.5); a stale record that shares the later boot's id (the Residual, §12.5) is detected as a splice unless its predecessor is byte-identical at the same position too.

**I-REPLAY-IDEM.** Replay is idempotent, so recovering twice, including a crash during recovery, gives the same state. Under (b), every op inserts a uniquely keyed entry (I-ENTRY-UNIQUE). Re-inserting an identical entry is a no-op, so partial applies already in the image (I-CKPT-AT-T (b)) are completed rather than duplicated. A chunked transaction's superseded ops replace one another in LSN order, so each pass ends with the last op per key (I-CHUNK-SUPERSEDE). CreateTree and DropTree are idempotent (§6.8). The final state is the set union of the entries, independent of insertion order. Pass 2 still runs in LSN order, so tree creation and drop ops come before and after the writes that depend on them. Mutants (one per direction of I-ENTRY-UNIQUE):
- **REPLAY-DUP-CONFLICT:** an identical re-insert is treated as a conflict (XX001);
- **DUP-DIFFERENT-ACCEPTED:** a re-insert with a different value is accepted (overwritten or ignored) instead of XX001.

The double-crash test (A-7d) is the test that runs recovery twice. It is not a mutant.

**I-REDO-COMPLETE.** Every acked `synchronous_commit=on` commit is either `≤ T` of the recovered image, and in it, or has durable records at or after `wal_start_lsn` before the valid end, and is replayed, given I-ACK-PREFIX (§12.4) and a device that keeps what a completed sync covered (above). This holds for a walk from an older meta after a meta fault too, because the walk crosses into newer boots' records (§12.5), when that meta's image and WAL span are intact and no replayed commit depends on a newer checkpoint (§12.8). The previous meta's image and WAL span are intact (I-TWO-GEN), and I-CKPT-DEP refuses the dependency. An intact image and WAL span are not enough alone: a load's unlogged entries are in no WAL. Otherwise X5-R5's detection (XX001 on the older image's reused slots, §3.3), I-WAL-START (a recycled start segment) or I-CKPT-DEP applies. Mutants:
- **START-LSN-PAST-CHUNK:** the replay start ignores an open transaction's first chunk;
- **START-LSN-MARKED-CHUNK:** the replay start counts only transactions with no marker. Its test is §12.6's case: a chunked transaction whose marker (`commit_ts > T`) lies after a commit `≤ T`, with its apply unfinished at the flip, then a crash after step 6. The transaction must be recovered whole.

**E4's oracle.** The recovered state equals the model at some commit `c`, with max acked sync-on commit `≤ c ≤` last WAL-written commit (PLAN-v3 §4 E4). That follows from I-REDO-COMPLETE, I-CKPT-AT-T, I-REPLAY-IDEM and I-WAL-TS-ORDER: the durable commits form a ts-prefix.

### 14.1 Read-only open (N11)
**Every build offers a read-only open**, in server, embedded and browser modes (PLAN-v3 §10 item 8): it is the only path to the data after WAL loss (below), and the export path of a stopped store (C4 decision 5). N11's single-owner rule is unchanged. A read-only open recovers exactly the state a read-write open recovers, but keeps it in memory and writes nothing, except where a read-write open fails and a read-only open serves salvage instead: WAL loss, a splice, a missing start segment, an unmet checkpoint dependency and an unproven fall-back (below; §14 steps 3, 3a, 3b and 4a).

**A stopped store opens read-only with zero free bytes (C4 decision 5).** A store that stopped on 53100 (a full volume or an OPFS quota) is, on disk, a crashed store, because poisoning lives only in memory (§9.2). Whatever the write point, a WAL append, a segment's creation or preallocation, a file extension (§4.5), a checkpoint page write or the meta write, the cut-short write lies past the valid end (§12.5), in a slot or extension that image `k` does not reference (§14 step 2), or in a meta place, where a cut-short copy is invalid and the open rule selects the newest meta with a valid copy (§2.2). So a read-only open of it **succeeds on a volume with zero free bytes** and writes nothing, provided its pool meets the evictable minimum for the replay (below). The data stays readable and exportable without freeing any space. Test `t_ro_open_disk_full` (A-7d with the walk scan S6; B-6 with A-4e's cursor; §15 I-RO-OPEN, §15.1).

**No writer.** While a read-only open is open, no read-write open of the file runs: it takes N11's file lock (through `Env`) in a mode that excludes a read-write open, and it fails rather than wait when a writer holds the lock. **The lock creates and writes no file**, in either mode: it is an advisory lock on the page file itself (opened read-only by a read-only open), or, on OPFS, the access-handle mode (`read-only` against a writer's exclusive `readwrite`). A lock file would be a write, and on a full volume its creation fails (mutant **RO-LOCK-FILE**). <!-- For A-1: an O_RDONLY fd cannot take a POSIX fcntl write lock, so the read-only open needs a shared (read) lock mode; PLAN-v3 §3.2's Env has only lock_exclusive (follow-up proposed in verdicts/S-2-F2.md). --> Why exclude a writer: a writer recycles WAL segments and reuses slots after every checkpoint (§13 step 6), so the reader's image and WAL span would change under it. Whether several read-only opens may share the file is the lock mode's choice; nothing here depends on it, because none of them writes.

**What it replays.** Steps 1–4, including the open-time meta check (step 1) and the coverage check (step 3a): the same winning meta (§2.2), the same image, the same valid end (§12.5), or the splice cut (§14 step 3a), or the dependency cut (step 3b), the same fall-back (step 4a), the same two passes, and the same ops, with I-CHUNK-SUPERSEDE. It builds the root table as §14 step 2 does, and its replayed directory changes maintain it.
- So its state equals the state a read-write open of the same files has after step 4, before its boot checkpoint.
- The apply frontier is fixed as after step 4 (the largest replayed `commit_ts`, or `T`), and `W` starts at the meta's `gc_w` (C-T0 v8 §3.7 steps 2–3). Neither advances, because nothing commits.
- Skipping pass 2 would serve image `k` alone and silently lose every acked commit above `T` (mutant **RO-NO-REPLAY**).

**WAL loss: salvage (I-WAL-COVER).** When the coverage check fails, a read-write open fails with XX001 (§14 step 3a), but a read-only open succeeds in **salvage**:
- It runs pass 2 over the valid span as usual and fixes the apply frontier at the largest replayed `commit_ts`, or `T`. `visible_ts` is that frontier, and no snapshot above it is admitted.
- **It serves the ts-prefix state at that frontier.**
  - Every commit `≤ T` is in the image (I-CKPT-AT-T (a)), and every commit in `(T, frontier]` is replayed from records that read back.
  - Every lost commit has a ts above the frontier (I-WAL-TS-ORDER), including the commits whose entries sit in the image without a record, and so does every bulk-load entry whose fence commit was lost. A read at `S` sees only versions `≤ S` (§8), so no read sees a lost commit.
  - Dead provisional entries stay dead.
- **Dropped trees.** The tree directory is unversioned. Image `k` can hold a `dropping` mark, and page frees, from a drop-cleanup commit `c_d` above `T`, even one whose record is lost: `c_d`'s apply sets `dropping`, and the bounded walk then runs as a background job (C-T6-rehost §7.4), with nothing waiting for `M ≥ c_d`. Salvage still never reads a freed page:
  - the drop job issues `c_d` only once `W > S_job`, where `S_job ≥ S_ddl`, the retiring DDL's commit ts (C-T6-rehost §7.4, C-T0 v8 §9.3);
  - `c_d` was applied before the flip, so it was issued before it;
  - `W ≤ M` at all times and `M` is monotone (C-T0 v8 §3.5), so `S_ddl < W ≤ T ≤` the salvage frontier;
  - a read at a salvage snapshot `S ≥ S_ddl` reaches trees only through the catalog at `S` (C-T5), which no longer names the dropped trees;
  - a `CreateTree` of a lost commit leaves a tree that no catalog at `S ≤` the frontier names;
  - the drop walk does not resume in a read-only open (below).

  Snapshots below `S_ddl` are governed here exactly as in any open, by the watermark floor (C-T0 v8 §3.7 step 3).
- It reports the loss with the open: the valid end, `wal_ckpt_end`, `T`, `ts_ckpt_hi` and the frontier.
- **The remedy** is to export from it into a new file (D-7). The read-write open stays refused, and nothing in 2.0 repairs the file in place.
- Mutant **SALVAGE-ABOVE-FRONTIER**: the salvage open admits snapshots above the frontier (for example, `visible_ts := ts_ckpt_hi`) and reads part of a lost commit. Test `t_wal_loss_salvage` (A-7d with the directory as the catalog's tree list (S3) and the walk scan S6; B-6 with the catalog and A-4e's cursor; §15.1).

**Splice: salvage (I-WAL-CHAIN).** A splice (§14 step 3a) is served in the same way, with the valid end cut back to the splice cut. Pass 2 replays only records below the cut. The apply frontier is fixed at the largest `commit_ts` replayed, or `T`, and no snapshot above it is admitted. So the open serves the ts-prefix at that frontier, by the argument above. The open reports the splice (the record's LSN, its `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`, the walk's predecessor's start and `crc`, and the cut) and the coverage result against the cut. The remedy is the same export. Mutant **SPLICE-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT**: salvage replays records at or after the cut. Test `t_wal_splice`, read-only variant (A-7d).

**Start segment, dependency and fall-back: salvage.** A missing start segment (§14 step 3, I-WAL-START) is served with the valid end at `wal_start_lsn`: pass 2 replays nothing, and the frontier is `T`. An unmet dependency (§14 step 3b, I-CKPT-DEP) is served with the valid end cut back to the dependency cut. An unproven fall-back (§14 step 4a, I-FALLBACK) is served from meta `k−1`, with whatever cut its own checks give. Where several cuts apply, the lowest one does. Each serves the ts-prefix at its frontier, by the argument above, and reports what cut it. A proven fall-back is not salvage: it serves the state a read-write open recovers. Mutant DEP-SALVAGE-PAST-CUT (§14 step 3b).

**Where the replay lives: in the pool, unwritten.** Pass 2's mutations, including its splits and the LPIDs they allocate, exist only in memory.
- **Eviction.** A dirty frame is never evicted, because §5.2's dirty-victim write is a write. Clean frames are evicted as usual.
- **Pool budget.** The evictable part of the pool (`F` minus the dirty frames) must meet §5.4's minimum, both during pass 2 and after it. When it would not, the open fails with 53200, having written nothing.
- **The remedy for 53200** is either a read-only open with a larger pool budget (`F` is set per open from the N16 budget, §5.1), which still writes nothing, or a read-write open, whose boot checkpoint makes the replay durable, so that a read-only open of the files it leaves replays nothing.
- **On a volume with no free space, the larger read-only open is the only guaranteed remedy.** A read-write open may need space for its boot checkpoint (§14 step 8), can fail there with 53100 before its meta is durable (§13), and leaves a store that recovers the same state as before.
- **Replay is the only mutation.** No GC mutation (§10), page-local prune, LPID free, directory change or checkpoint runs. A replayed `DropTree` only sets its entry's state (§14 step 4), so the tree's pages stay mapped (mutant REPLAY-DROP-WALK).

**What it skips.**
- **Step 5:** the WAL is not truncated. The torn tail stays on disk, every walk stops at the same valid end (§12.5), and the next read-write open truncates it.
- **Step 6:** `dropping` walks do not resume. Each such tree is in the state a read-write open has before its B-6 job runs.
- **Step 7:** amcheck may run, because it only reads.
- **Step 8:** the boot checkpoint does not run, and there is no boot id. Every provisional entry, from any boot, is dead (C-T0 v8 §2.4), and every read ignores it from its bytes, with no lookup (§8). The live-txn registry stays empty.

**What it refuses.**
- **Every write through `Env`** on the page file or `wal/`: no write, sync, rename, truncate, preallocation, segment recycling or creation. A missing file fails the open, since §2.3's creation is a write. Mutant **RO-WRITES**: the open truncates the WAL (step 5), writes an evicted replayed frame, resumes a drop walk, checkpoints, or retires a meta (§14 step 4a).
- **Every write transaction.** Every transaction is READ ONLY, and a write gets 25006 (C-T0 v8 §2.5).
- **A corrupt meta**, and a newest image that fails at open with no previous image, fail as they do for a read-write open (XX001, §2.2, §3.3, §14 step 4a). A newest image that fails with a previous image is served from it (§14 step 4a).

**No trace.** Closing or crashing a read-only open leaves every file byte-identical. A later read-write open recovers the same state it would have recovered without it.

### 14.2 Outcomes at open
A summary of §2.2, §14 and §14.1; where it and they differ, they rule.

| Case | Read-write open | Read-only open |
|---|---|---|
| No valid meta copy; page 0 invalid and copies of two identities; page 0 and both replicas invalid (§2.2) | XX001 | XX001 |
| Every meta place unreadable (§2.2) | 58030 | 58030 |
| A misplaced copy newer than the newest meta (I-META-SLOT) | XX001 | XX001 |
| A tie with equal `boot_id`s (§2.2) | XX001 | XX001 |
| The newest meta fails step 1's range check | XX001, with no fall-back | XX001 |
| The start segment missing (step 3, I-WAL-START) | XX001, nothing written | salvage at `T` |
| WAL loss (step 3a, I-WAL-COVER) | XX001, nothing written | salvage at the valid end |
| A splice (step 3a, I-WAL-CHAIN) | XX001, nothing written | salvage at the splice cut |
| An unmet dependency (step 3b, I-CKPT-DEP) | XX001, nothing written | salvage at the dependency cut |
| The newest image fails at open, with a previous image (its free set read at step 2), and the proof holds (step 4a) | opens from meta `k−1`, retiring meta `k` first, and reports | the same state, nothing written |
| The same, and the proof fails | XX001, nothing written beyond step 4a's pass-2 evictions; an explicit rollback opens it | salvage from meta `k−1` |
| The same, and a check against meta `k−1` refuses | that check's refusal | that check's salvage |
| The same, with no previous image (no previous meta, or its free set unread at step 2), or image `k−1` failing too | XX001 | XX001 |
| An explicit rollback whose precondition does not hold | 55000, nothing written | (read-write only) |
| Otherwise | opens meta `k` | opens meta `k` |

---

## 15. Invariants

Each invariant needs a test and a named mutant that the test catches (PLAN-v3 §3.3, §8 phase A gate). The "Pinned by" column names the card that writes the test. These are P-written tests for the cards PLAN-v3 §7.1 lists (A-3, A-4, A-7, B-1, B-2). Mutant names taken from `verdicts/X-2.md` keep their meaning there.

**Stand-ins.** A test runs on the card §15.1 names, in the form it states. Where a fixture or a pass condition names machinery that lands on a later card, the earlier card runs a stated stand-in form, and §15.1 names the card that runs the full form, or the row says why its stand-in form is the test's last form (S1's and S5's rules; `t_epoch_reserve_two_exhausters` at A-7a). Only these stand-ins are allowed:
- **S1, the stand-in flip.** Before A-7c, "a flip" or "a flip pending" in a fixture is a test-only thread that takes the epoch gate exclusively (§13 step 0, item 1), records the map's root and its level and sets `e := k+1`, as item 2's last sentence does (S1 need not record the boot id, which only step 4 writes), and releases the gate (item 3), without the rest of item 2. A test whose mutants all fail through a hook, the latch tracker, a deadlock or the step budget is judged in full under S1, because none of its mutants touches what S1 leaves out of item 2.
- **S2, the read-back.** Before its card has a meta (A-7c), recovery (A-7d) or amcheck (A-8), a pass condition that names amcheck, an image that "opens", or "a read at `T` after a crash" is judged by reading back directly through the `Env`:
  - image `k`'s map from the slot step 2 records for its root;
  - each page from the slot that map names, with its CRC checked and its content compared with the oracle's epoch-`k` content;
  - for I-LPID-EXACT, the mapped LPIDs against the pages a test walk reaches from the roots;
  - for I-WAL-COVER, meta `k`'s `wal_ckpt_end` against the valid end A-6b's walk finds (§12.5).
- **S3, a test-only caller or input.** Where a store call's real caller, or an input the store reads, lands on a later card, the test makes the call or supplies the input as that caller will. The cases are:
  - `write_guard`'s first touch, copy and install, which call the reserve's draw and claim (§5.6);
  - settlers (a) and (c), `ROLLBACK TO` and replay, which call A-5's replace and delete (§6.7) or A-4e's cursor close (§6.6);
  - the apply frontier `M` and `visible_ts` (C-T0 v8);
  - GC's `gc_w_used` (§10.5);
  - a bulk load's fence commit, unlogged inserts and publishing commit, which carries the `ckpt_id` its forced checkpoint returned (§12.8);
  - an allocation restart's call that grows the map (§4.1 "Growth"), made as A-4a's restart will make it;
  - step 6's release of pending slots (§13 step 6), made as A-7c's checkpoint will make it;
  - a drop-cleanup commit's record;
  - a gated operation made to fail with XX001 (§9.2);
  - an owner's rewrite of its own provisional tag-8 entry (B-1d's write path), which calls A-5's replace (§6.7);
  - an `on` ack (A-6c's group commit), taken as the WAL writer's report that the record is synced (§12.4);
  - a meta written by the test with A-2's codec after step 2, where the card's Depends closure lacks A-7c's meta write (A-8's fixtures);
  - the catalog's list of trees at `S` (B-4a), taken from the directory at `S` (A-7b).
- **S4, the test-only sweep step.** A step that names its tree's root through §6.8's root names, with the shape of one §10.4 sweep step. A-7b writes it, and A-7d's tests share it.
- **S5, a second tree before A-7b.** A second root in the same pool, made by a test-only call. Directory and root-table behaviour stays A-7b's. A fixture run on S5 is judged in full when every mutant it judges fails through the latch tracker, a hook or a deadlock and the fixture names no root through the root table or the directory: latch ranks and holds do not depend on how the tree was made, and none of those mutants is applied to directory or root-table code. `t_latch_scope`'s two-tree fixtures are judged in full under this rule (§15.1).
- **S6, the walk scan.** Before a card's Depends closure has A-4e's cursor, "a full scan of every published tree" (§14.1), or of every tree the catalog at `S` names, is a test walk through the open store: from each tree's root, named through §6.8's root names, it reads every reachable page through the store's own page reads and decodes every entry of every leaf, and it succeeds when every read and decode does. A-7d's tests write it. S6 replaces a check, not code, as S2 does: a mutant judged under S6 counts at the owner, and the card that §15.1 names for the full form, which scans with A-4e's cursor, judges it again. No cursor mutant is judged under S6.

A mutant counts where it is applied to the real code it names. Applied to a stand-in's own code it proves the fixture only, as ROOT-NAME-KEPT does on A-7d's stand-in. A forged input, a value no caller produces, set to make a check fire (as `t_cover_ts_cross_check` forges metas), is fault injection, not a stand-in: a mutant that removes the check counts on the card whose code the check is. S2 replaces a check, not code, so a mutant of the owner's code that is judged under S2 counts at the owner, and the card that §15.1 names for the full form judges it again. A form that §15.1 names to a Phase B card is pinned by that card (PLAN-v3 §8, Phase A gate).
