Test `t_cursor_park_equiv` (A-4e on stand-ins, with A-4b packs racing; full forms B-3, B-1d, B-2c, R-1; §15.1): a scan parks after every entry while writers add versions above `S`, a GC sweep packs and merges its leaves, and another tree is dropped and recreated on reused LPIDs; its output equals the oracle scan at `S`, and in the SER variant so do its recorded edges. A mid-group variant: a group spans several leaves, its head is a provisional entry whose owner is `Committed(c)` with `c ≤ S`, finalize is held back, and the per-call bound fires while the scan skips the versions above `S`; a point-read variant runs a SER point read over the same group, finalize still held back, with a bound smaller than the group. A scroll variant fetches forward, parks, fetches backward, parks and fetches forward again; each fetch equals the oracle's from the same boundary. A close variant closes a parked cursor through `ROLLBACK TO`; its batch buffer is freed and R-1's per-session account returns to its value before the open. At A-4e the forms run on stand-ins: in the main form coupled deletes pack and merge the scan's leaves (A-4b, §6.5) and a split then takes each freed LPID (§4.2's lowest-first), so a remembered LPID names another page, and the output equals the oracle scan at `S`; the mid-group form runs over a group of ordinary versions that spans several leaves, with the per-call bound firing inside it (i) after the newest version `≤ S` was found and (ii) while the scan still skips versions above `S`, and its output equals the oracle's, one version per key; the point-read form runs a SER-mode point read over the same group, in cases (i) and (ii), with a bound smaller than the group, and returns the oracle's version; the scroll form runs as written; the close form closes a parked cursor through the store's close call, which `ROLLBACK TO` will use (S3), and a test hook sees its batch buffer freed; each resume goes through the tree's root LPID, which never changes (I-ROOT-STABLE). At A-4e, PARK-DROPS-CANDIDATE is applied to the found flag that a park inside a group carries (§6.6), so A-4e carries that flag in the parked position and does not recompute it from the resume key; recomputed, the flag leaves the mutant nothing to drop at A-4e. A resume that keeps only the key takes the newest version `≤ S` either as found, which fails case (ii) because the key's version is missing, or as not found, which fails case (i) because a second, older version of the key is returned, where A-4e parks inside the group after a find; where it parks after the group instead (§6.6), that second reading is equivalent at A-4e. The mutant's other half, the head's candidate version, is judged at B-1d. CURSOR-STALE-LPID (resume by a remembered LPID) fails it; PARK-DROPS-CANDIDATE (a mid-group park keeps only the resume key) fails the mid-group and point-read variants, which return a version older than `c`; SCROLL-RESUME-SKIP (a direction change resumes past the boundary) fails the scroll variant; BATCH-KEPT-AFTER-CLOSE (a closed cursor's batch buffer is not freed) fails the close variant | A-4e (with A-4b packs racing; stand-ins), B-3, B-1d, B-2c, R-1 (full forms, §15.1) | CURSOR-STALE-LPID, PARK-DROPS-CANDIDATE, SCROLL-RESUME-SKIP, BATCH-KEPT-AFTER-CLOSE |
| I-TREE | §6.1: key order and subtree bounds after every SMO | A-4a/A-4b (proptest vs `BTreeMap`) | SPLIT-OFF-BY-ONE, SEP-NOT-UPDATED, PACK-SEP-STALE |
| I-LATCH | §5.3/§6.2: coupling; one order (directory pages only by a directory change, then one tree's pages by depth and left to right, then map pages by radix rank), in which a thread blocks only on a latch ranked after every latch it holds, a claim takes no map latch and a map page's write never holds the page while it latches the parent; one call and one tree per latch hold, with two stated exceptions (`try_latch` acquirers, a stale root name's first latch); task-fair latches with bounded bypass; no recursive latch; no page read in a reader's hold. §6.8 "Root names": only an operation that changes the directory latches a directory page, and a root name comes from the root table and is validated under the root's latch. Test `t_dir_root_no_stall` (A-7b, with A-4c's shuttle harness and the stand-in step S4; B-3 with its sweep; §15.1), a regression for the directory leaving the hot path: trees X, D and Y have their entries in one directory leaf. A chain operation holds X's leaf root; a sweep step of D that named D's root while D was `live` is paused under that root's latch, while DropTree(D)'s last step holds the directory leaf exclusively and is held up on D's root; a CreateTree queues on that leaf. A reader and a writer of Y finish within the step budget under every schedule, and a hook asserts that no operation that does not change the directory latches a directory page. DIR-LATCH-ON-READ (an operation that does not change the directory latches a directory page) fails it through the hook, deterministically. Test `t_freeset_lock_order` (A-3c, with A-4c's shuttle harness): allocations pin MapLeafs under the free-set lock while dirty evictions allocate slots and a pool at its minimum forces eviction; its fixture evicts the MapLeaf of the lowest free LPID, and a variant has no free LPID and `next_lpid` at a MapLeaf boundary. A hook asserts no page read, eviction, page creation or frame latch taken while the lock is held (the allocation already holds `P`'s), and no take of the free-set lock by a thread holding the page table, and every schedule finishes. FREESET-LOCK-IO (an allocation creates or reads a MapLeaf under the lock) and FREESET-LOCK-INVERT (a dirty eviction takes the free-set lock while holding the page table) fail it. Test `t_latch_scope` (A-4c; its cursor fixtures and reverse variant A-4e, with A-4c's tracker; §15.1): a per-thread latch tracker asserts, at every storage-call return and every yield point, that the thread holds no latch and no pin; at every blocking acquisition of a tree page, that the thread holds no latch of another tree; and at every blocking acquisition, that the thread holds no latch ranked at or after the one it requests (§5.3 "Order": map pages rank after every tree page, and pages of different trees are unranked). Fixtures: an index scan with table lookups, a self-join behind a queued writer, two cursors that each read the other's tree, and two threads that each hold a leaf exclusively and read the other's tree. A reverse variant runs a reverse scan across a left-to-right pack. A map variant runs a pool at its minimum through a checkpoint, so that dirty MapLeafs are evicted and step 2's map phase runs, while two writers that each hold a MapLeaf's latch in `write_guard` exhaust the reserve; a hook pauses one MapLeaf's eviction after it releases the MapLeaf's latch and before it sets the parent's ref, while another thread looks up an LPID whose entry in that MapLeaf changed since its old slot. In the same pause the evicted MapLeaf is a previous-epoch victim (§5.2) of checkpoint `k`, and step 2's map phase builds it from its frame; a hook asserts that the map phase sets no current ref. A checkpoint variant has step 2 wait on a frame whose holder records into a MapLeaf. A floor variant runs a pool at its minimum, a gated writer queued on a root with a flip pending, and a holder of that root that needs a frame. Every schedule finishes. CURSOR-LATCH-ACROSS-CALL and CURSOR-PIN-ACROSS-CALL (a cursor keeps a latch or a pin past the call's return) and LATCH-ACROSS-YIELD (a call yields holding a latch) fail it through the tracker; NESTED-READ-UNDER-LATCH (a thread reads another tree, or re-latches its own, while holding latches) fails it through the tracker and deadlocks the two-leaf and self-join fixtures; REVERSE-LATCH-LEFT (a reverse cursor latches the previous leaf while holding its leaf) deadlocks the reverse variant; MAP-PARENT-UNDER-CHILD (a map page's write latches its parent while holding the page) fails the map variant through the tracker; MAP-DROP-BEFORE-REF (an evicted map page leaves the page table before its parent's ref is set) fails the map variant: the paused lookup reloads the old slot and returns a stale entry; MAP-REF-SET-STALE (the map phase makes the slot it writes a map page's current home) fails the map variant through the hook, and the paused eviction's ref then names a slot already reusable; CKPT-WAIT-HOLDING-MAP (step 2 waits on a frame while holding a map latch) deadlocks the checkpoint variant; POOL-FLOOR-BLOCK (a thread holding a latch waits for an unpin instead of releasing and restarting) deadlocks the floor variant. Test `t_root_latch_fair` (A-3b, with A-4c's shuttle harness; its cold variant A-4a, re-run with a cursor at A-4e, §15.1): a stream of overlapping readers holds tree X's root shared while a writer queues; the writer is granted within a bypass budget, a bound with slack on the later requests granted before it, not their arrival order, so a strict-FIFO latch passes too. A writer variant runs a stream of writers on X's root while a reader queues; the reader is granted within the same budget. A latch-level variant drives the latch type directly: with a writer queued on a frame that a reader holds shared, `try_latch` in either mode fails, including at the instant the reader releases. A cold variant has a reader under X's root meet a non-resident child while a gated writer queues on the root with a flip pending; a hook asserts that no reader reads a page while it holds a latch, and the flip completes within the step budget. LATCH-READER-PREF (a waiting writer does not block new shared grants) fails the reader stream; LATCH-WRITER-PREF (writers always overtake waiting readers) fails the writer variant; TRYLATCH-BYPASS (`try_latch` succeeds over a queued exclusive waiter) fails the latch-level variant; READER-IO-UNDER-LATCH (a reader loads a page while holding its parent's latch) fails the cold variant through the hook | A-4c (loom/shuttle, `t_latch_scope`'s tracker, two-leaf, map, checkpoint and floor forms), A-4e (`t_latch_scope`'s cursor fixtures and reverse variant), A-3b (`t_root_latch_fair`), A-4a (its cold variant), A-4e (the cold variant with a cursor), A-3c (`t_freeset_lock_order`), A-7b (`t_dir_root_no_stall`), B-3 (`t_dir_root_no_stall` on its sweep) | COUPLING-EARLY-RELEASE, PACK-RIGHT-FIRST, DIR-LATCH-ON-READ, FREESET-LOCK-IO, FREESET-LOCK-INVERT, CURSOR-LATCH-ACROSS-CALL, CURSOR-PIN-ACROSS-CALL, NESTED-READ-UNDER-LATCH, LATCH-ACROSS-YIELD, REVERSE-LATCH-LEFT, MAP-PARENT-UNDER-CHILD, MAP-DROP-BEFORE-REF, MAP-REF-SET-STALE, CKPT-WAIT-HOLDING-MAP, POOL-FLOOR-BLOCK, LATCH-READER-PREF, LATCH-WRITER-PREF, TRYLATCH-BYPASS, READER-IO-UNDER-LATCH |
| I-ROOT-STABLE | §6.3: a tree's root LPID never changes | A-4a/A-4b (in process), A-7d (reopen; §15.1) | ROOT-MOVES |
| I-PACK | §6.5: entries preserved; merge iff it fits in `F_PACK·U`; never splits | A-4b | PACK-DROP-LAST, PACK-OVERFILL |
| I-ENTRY-UNIQUE / I-REPLAY-IDEM | §9.1, §14: identical re-insert (chains compared by content) is a no-op, a different one XX001; recovering twice gives the same state | B-1c, A-7d (incl. the double-crash test; an overflow value in a partially applied commit) | REPLAY-DUP-CONFLICT, DUP-DIFFERENT-ACCEPTED, CHAIN-LPID-COMPARED |
| I-CHUNK-SUPERSEDE | §12.7, §14: within one chunked transaction the last op per key wins, `revert` deletes; replay twice gives the same state | A-7d, B-1d | SUPERSEDE-AS-CONFLICT |
| I-KEY-SIZE | §3.2: an entry key over `K_MAX` is refused with 54000 at write-set entry; apply never sees one | A-2 (formula and values), B-1a (the write path calls the check) | SIZE-CHECK-OFF |
| I-FAIL-STOP | §9.2: a 58030/53100/53200/XX001 inside a gated operation, a checkpoint step, a background store job or the WAL path (a WAL write or `sync_data`, a segment's creation, preallocation, recycling rename or `wal/` directory sync, recovery's step-5 truncation) poisons the store; a client read's XX001 never does. The mark is made before the first release, and every acquisition checks it. Every blocking wait, the lock-table waits included, has it in its predicate, under the wait's own mutex, and sleeps by releasing that mutex in one step, re-testing on every wake. The broadcast comes after every latch, pin, the gate, the settle stripe and every internal mutex are released, and reaches every wait queue, each live transaction's waiter list included. After poison, every call fails, every waiter returns, and nothing is written. Test `t_gated_xx001_poisons`: a damaged referenced leaf on an apply's path; every commit waiter returns the error within the test's step budget, and no task is left parked. Its **lock-waiter variant**: a txn waits in the lock table on a key held by a commit that sits behind the poisoned apply, so the holder is never released; the waiter must return the error (POISON-LOST-WAKEUP and POISON-WAITER-PARKED leave it parked; pinned in B-1c and B-1b). Its **WAL variant**: the group-commit writer's `sync_data` fails (58030); the store is poisoned and every waiter on `durable_end` returns the error. WAL-ERR-NOT-POISON (an error on the WAL path does not poison the store) fails it. Test `t_client_read_xx001_no_poison` (B-1c): a client read of that leaf gets XX001, then an unrelated commit and a checkpoint both succeed. Test `t_poison_before_unlatch` (shuttle; A-3c with an evictor and a stand-in operation, A-4c with a reader and a split; §15.1): a reader that races the failing SMO gets the error, never the half-changed page; so does an evictor's `try_latch`. Test `t_poison_wakeup_race` (shuttle; A-4c, B-1c): a waiter tests its predicate concurrently with the poisoner's mark and broadcast; under every schedule it returns the error. POISON-LOST-WAKEUP (a wait predicate omits the mark, so a woken waiter re-tests only its own condition and sleeps again) fails it, and so does a broadcast made before the mark | B-1c (commit waiters, COMMIT's error, the WAL variant); B-1b, the lock-table card, with B-2b for `wait_for` (the lock-waiter variant); A-3c, A-4c (the latch and `try_latch` check, shuttle) | POISON-IGNORED, XX001-NOT-POISON, CLIENT-READ-POISONS, POISON-WAITER-PARKED, POISON-AFTER-UNLATCH, POISON-LOST-WAKEUP, WAL-ERR-NOT-POISON |
| I-GC-READEQ | §10.2: every GC mutation is read-equivalent at every S ≥ the published W, including a sweep's own delete of each chain-bearing entry (§10.4, "Chains first"). Test `t_sweep_step_shape` (B-3): a sweep over a tree of height ≥ 2 with a toast-region leaf and a leaf of maximum-size values, each holding superseded chain-bearing versions and dead tag-8 entries next to non-chain garbage, with every chain evicted, reads at `S ≥ W` between its gated operations, and a hook that counts each gated operation's chain frees and, at each chain page read, checks the latches the thread holds. The fixture also holds (a) an elided-tombstone group that ends within the step's `L` and `R` and whose older versions hold chains, with no version above `w`, read at `S ≥ W` between each two of the gated operations that delete its entries; and (b) a writer, run by a hook between the step's end and the delete of a dead tag-8 entry on key `k`, that settles that entry (settler (c)) and writes its own current-boot tag-8 entry on `k`, then stays `Open`. An operation frees at most one chain, no chain page is read under an inner page's latch, the step that packs frees no chain, every read is unchanged, the writer's entry and its chain survive the sweep (its owner reads its own value, and amcheck finds the chain mapped and referenced), and every read of the tombstone's key at `S ≥ W` finds no version. SWEEP-CHAINS-UNDER-P (a step deletes its chain-bearing entries and frees their chains in its own mutation, under `P`'s latch) fails it through the hook. CHAIN-DELETE-BY-KEY (the delete of a marked chain-bearing entry matches its entry key alone, with no identity check) fails it through (b): it deletes the writer's entry and frees its chain. STEP-MUTATE-BEFORE-CHAINS (the step deletes its non-chain marks, the ending group's tombstone among them, in its mutation, and only then deletes the chain-bearing ones) fails it through (a): a read between the tombstone's delete and an older version's sees that version | B-3 (+ G0 re-point, B-7) | GC-DROPS-VISIBLE, G-WATERMARK+1, G-RESURRECT, SWEEP-CHAINS-UNDER-P, CHAIN-DELETE-BY-KEY |
| I-GC-ORDER | §10.2/§10.4: an elided tombstone is deleted after every older version of its key, including the chain-bearing ones a sweep deletes in their own gated operations (§10.4, "Chains first") | B-3 (a group spanning leaves, read between sweep steps or across a checkpoint and crash; `t_sweep_step_shape`'s fixture (a), I-GC-READEQ row) | G-TOMB-FIRST, STEP-MUTATE-BEFORE-CHAINS |
| I-GC-COMPLETE | §10.2: after a quiescent sweep, at most one version ≤ w per key, and it is live | B-3 (the §10.2 directed test, with a reader) | GC-KEEPS-TOMB |
| I-GC-LOCAL | §10.3: page-local prune acts only on what the leaf proves; it frees no chain and elides a tombstone only when no older version of its group holds one. Test `t_prune_split_shape` (B-3): a tree of height ≥ 2 whose target leaf is a full toast-region leaf. Its superseded chunk versions (tag 4) each have a newer `≤ w` version on the leaf, and the leaf also holds dead tag-8 entries, an elided-tombstone candidate group that ends on the leaf with a tag-4 older version, non-chain garbage, and every chain evicted. An insert into that leaf prunes and splits. A hook counts each gated operation's chain frees and LPID frees and, at each page read, checks the latches the thread holds. The splitting insert frees no chain and no LPID and reads no Overflow page. Every chain of a pruned-around version stays mapped and referenced (amcheck). A read of the tombstone's key at `S ≥ W` after the prune still finds the tombstone. A sweep afterwards deletes each chain-bearing entry in its own gated operation, and I-GC-COMPLETE holds after it. Its eviction variant runs the prune at eviction of the same leaf, nested inside another tree's gated insert, with the same checks. PRUNE-FREES-CHAINS (the prune deletes a chain-bearing version or a dead tag-8 entry and frees its chain in the splitting insert's gated operation) fails it through the hook: it counts a chain free, and an Overflow page read under `P`'s exclusive latch. PRUNE-ELIDES-OVER-CHAIN (the prune skips a group's chain-bearing older version and still elides the group's tombstone) fails it through the read of the tombstone's key at `S ≥ W`, which finds the older version (I-GC-READEQ, I-GC-ORDER). PRUNE-EVICT-FREES-CHAIN (only the split-time prune skips chains; the prune at eviction still frees them, under the outer operation's hold) fails its eviction variant: the hook counts a chain free inside the outer insert | B-3 | PRUNE-FIRST-ENTRY, PRUNE-FREES-CHAINS, PRUNE-ELIDES-OVER-CHAIN, PRUNE-EVICT-FREES-CHAIN |
| I-SPACE | §11: quiescent fill bound at every height, except rightmost children and the reported `skips`; B-3's W4-shape acceptance ≤ 1.5× in tree bytes (reachable pages × `page_size`) | A-4b (bound, incl. trees of height ≥ 4), B-3 (acceptance) | PACK-OFF, SKIP-UNCOUNTED, SWEEP-PACK-LEFT, CASCADE-ONE-LEVEL, JUNCTION-SKIP |
| I-GCW | §10.5: meta `gc_w` bounds the image's GC and is `≤ T` | A-7c (both mutants; GCW-BOUND-SKIP by a forged `gc_w_used > T`), B-3 (restart floor; §15.1) | GCW-NOT-RECORDED, GCW-BOUND-SKIP |
| I-LPID-EXACT | §6.8: in every image, crash images included, mapped LPIDs = reachable pages; no leak on pack, drop or chain free. "Quiescent" describes amcheck's reading, not an exemption | A-8 amcheck, A-4b (in process; S2), B-6, A-7d (images; §15.1) | LEAK-ON-PACK, DROP-ENTRY-SPLIT |
| I-LINK-ATOMIC | §5.6, §6.7, §6.8, §12.8, §14 step 4: every gated operation leaves the reference graph closed. An LPID is mapped in the gated operation that inserts its first reference and unmapped in the one that removes its last, after removing it, so every image satisfies I-LPID-EXACT with no special case. An operation restarts only before its first mutation. DropTree deletes each chain-bearing entry with its chain in its own gated operation; its last step removes the entry, then frees the root, in one gated operation; one walk runs per tree; a root LPID is named through the root table, validated under the root's latch and not kept (§6.8); pass 2 runs no walk. The walk's code is A-7b's; A-7d's tests call it directly, and B-6 runs it as the drop job and the boot resume. A split reserves its pages and its allocations' MapLeafs before its prune and decides from the prune's marks whether it splits (§6.4); an allocation pins a resident MapLeaf or restarts (§5.6); a replace, a settle or a chain-freeing delete outside the sweep frees its old chain holding only the leaf (§6.7); a chain build holds only the leaf (§6.7). Test `t_drop_every_cut` (A-7d, B-6): a flip at every boundary between a DropTree's gated steps, with and without a concurrent split that takes the freed LPID. Its fixture holds chain-bearing entries in a non-root leaf and in a leaf root, an evicted directory page with a flip pending, an evicted root and an evicted MapLeaf of an LPID the walk frees, each with a flip pending, in the last step and in an unlink step, a running sweep of the tree paused between its steps until the walk ends, and a second DropTree during the walk. Every image passes amcheck and opens, no other tree loses a page, and one walk runs. DROP-ENTRY-SPLIT (directory root, unmap; E4's probe: 28 of 51 C-XX rounds), DROP-UNLINK-SPLIT (child pointer, unmap), DROP-RESTART-MID-OP (the flip pending lands in its restart), DROP-RESTART-AFTER-REMOVE and UNLINK-RESTART-AFTER-UNLINK, which are DROP-RESTART-MID-OP's realistic form (a forgotten pin: the flip lands in the restart, and the image fails I-LPID-EXACT, amcheck finding a mapped, unreachable page), ROOT-NAME-KEPT (the paused sweep then prunes another tree's page) and DROP-SECOND-WALK fail it. At A-7d the paused sweep is S4, the stand-in step A-7b writes, which follows §6.8's root names; `t_drop_step_shape` runs on the same fixture and shares it. ROOT-NAME-KEPT applied to the stand-in proves the fixture only. At B-6 both tests re-run with B-3's real sweep, and ROOT-NAME-KEPT is applied to B-3's sweep code and must fail there. A-7d's kill does not count toward the sweep. Test `t_drop_step_shape` (A-7d, B-6): on the same fixture, a hook counts each gated operation's frees and page reads, and at each LPID free reads the pages the operation holds. An operation frees at most one chain, an unlink step or a last step frees none and reads no page after its first mutation, and no held page or entry names the LPID being freed. DROP-LEAF-CHAINS and DROP-FREE-BEFORE-REMOVE fail it. Its fixture also holds a directory leaf that the removal leaves below `U/4`, and the hook asserts that the last step latches no directory page beside its own leaf; DROP-LAST-STEP-PACKS fails it. Test `t_split_reserve_order` (A-4a): a full leaf with prunable non-chain garbage, too little to fit the insert's cell, under a height-≥ 2 tree, the MapLeaf of the next free LPID evicted, and a flip pending; a variant whose split also splits `P` evicts the MapLeaf of its second LPID. A hook at each gate release and each page read asserts that no restart follows a mutation and no page is read under an inner exclusive latch. SPLIT-RESERVE-AFTER-PRUNE (the split prunes, then decides to split or reserves, meets the evicted MapLeaf and restarts, releasing the gate) and ALLOC-READ-UNDER-P (the allocation reads its evicted MapLeaf in place under `P`'s exclusive latch) fail it through the hook. Its second variant has a full leaf whose marks free just enough room for the insert's cell: the hook asserts that the insert allocates no LPID and pins no MapLeaf. SPLIT-RESERVE-ALWAYS fails it there: an insert into an unsafe leaf allocates and pins its split's reservation before it knows it splits. Test `t_alloc_restart_progress` (A-4a, with A-3c; the race variant's image check A-7d; §15.1): a split whose allocation restarts; during each restart a hook frees a lower LPID under an evicted MapLeaf. The split finishes within the restart bound §5.6 states. A variant has no free LPID, `next_lpid` at a MapLeaf boundary and every MapLeaf evicted; the hook asserts no page is read or created under an inner exclusive latch. ALLOC-RETRY-LOWEST (the retry takes the lowest free LPIDs afresh) fails the first; ALLOC-NEW-MAPLEAF-IN-PLACE (the allocation creates the new MapLeaf under `P`'s exclusive latch) fails the variant. A race variant restarts two allocations on the same missing MapLeaf, with a pool at its minimum so that obtaining a frame evicts a dirty page: the first creates the MapLeaf, and its retry maps an LPID there before the second latches the MapInner; the second finds the ref set, pins that MapLeaf and maps the next LPID. Every image passes amcheck, both LPIDs stay mapped, and the hook asserts that no page is evicted or read under the MapInner's exclusive latch. MAPLEAF-CREATE-UNCONDITIONAL (the second creates a MapLeaf anyway and replaces the ref) fails it through amcheck (I-LPID-EXACT); MAPLEAF-FRAME-UNDER-INNER (the frame is obtained after the MapInner is latched) fails it through the hook. Test `t_replace_free_shape` (A-5, with A-4a's split, on stand-ins; A-7d with images and replay; §15.1): under a height-≥ 2 tree, a full leaf whose owner rewrites its provisional tag-8 entry with a larger value (so the leaf is unsafe and splits), and a second unsafe leaf where the rewrite fits without a split; the old chain's pages and the MapLeafs of its LPIDs evicted, and a flip pending. Variants: a writer on `k` settles a dead tag-8 entry with a chain (settler (c)), and a superseding replay op replaces a chain-bearing entry. Delete variants, each on a full leaf under the same height-≥ 2 tree with the tag-8 entry's chain pages and their MapLeafs evicted and a flip pending: settler (a)'s abort-cleanup delete, `T`'s `ROLLBACK TO` delete, and a replayed `Delete{revert}` of a tag-8 entry. A build variant, under the same height-≥ 2 tree: the rewritten entry's old value is inline, so nothing is freed; its new value needs a chain, and the rewrite splits the leaf but not `P`. The split takes the last LPID its MapLeaf covers, every lower LPID mapped, so the next free LPIDs the new chain takes lie on later MapLeafs, each evicted, unpinned and mapping no dirty page (§4.1), with a flip pending. Those MapLeafs are the only pages the variant reads cold after the split. A hook at each page read and each gate release asserts that no page is read under an inner exclusive latch and no restart follows a mutation, and every image passes amcheck. REPLACE-FREE-UNDER-P (the insert frees the old chain while still holding `P`) fails it through the hook: a chain page or MapLeaf is read under `P`'s exclusive latch. DELETE-FREE-UNDER-P (a chain-freeing delete outside the sweep frees its chain while still holding `P`) fails a delete variant through the hook: a chain page or MapLeaf is read under `P`'s exclusive latch. CHAIN-BUILD-UNDER-P (a chain build after a split keeps `P` and reads its allocations' MapLeafs under it) fails the build variant through the hook: an evicted MapLeaf is read under `P`'s exclusive latch. Test `t_create_every_cut` (B-6): the leak direction; under CREATE-TREE-SPLIT (directory root, map), I-LPID-EXACT fails after recovery. Test `t_chain_every_cut` (A-7d): a flip at every boundary between the gated operations that insert, replace and delete chain-bearing entries, with an evicted leaf and a flip pending; every image passes amcheck and opens. CHAIN-BUILD-SPLIT (chain reference, map: a chain mapped one gated step before its entry, such as a build before the descent and then a restart), CHAIN-FREE-SPLIT (chain reference, unmap: a chain freed one gated step after its entry's delete), CHAIN-NEXT-MAP-SPLIT (next pointer, map: an Overflow page mapped one gated step before its predecessor's pointer) and CHAIN-NEXT-FREE-SPLIT (next pointer, unmap: an Overflow page freed one gated step before its predecessor) fail it. Test `t_load_every_cut` (B-4d): a flip at every boundary between a load's gated steps, then a crash; every image passes amcheck, and walking the discarded build leaks nothing. LOAD-PAGE-SPLIT (child pointer, map) fails it. Test `t_ro_open` with a replayed `DropTree` (A-7d): under REPLAY-DROP-WALK, a read-only open frees a page. Test `t_root_name_reuse` (A-7b, with A-4c's harness): tree X has root `r`, and no LPID below `r` is free, so §4.2's lowest-first order hands `r` to the next allocation; a fixture check asserts that the reusing tree's root or page is at `r`, and an oracle records each LPID's owning tree at every allocation and free. Case A: thread A reads X's entry and a hook stops it before it pins `r`; DropTree(X) runs to completion, CreateTree(Z) takes `r` as Z's root, and A resumes. Case B: A names X's root in a test-only mode that accepts `dropping`, a stand-in as A-7d's paused sweep is; the hook is in DropTree(X)'s last step, after it releases the root's latch and before the operation ends, and there a split of tree Y, in its own gated operation, takes `r` before A names X's root. Case C: as case A, but `r` is freed and not reused, so A's pin finds `r` unmapped. Case D: as case A, but `r` is reused as a non-root page of tree Y, and in a variant as an Overflow page of Y. In a second variant a hook pauses Y's split after `r` is mapped, to a frame that last held another LPID's page, and before Y initialises that page; A latches the frame there if the allocation path allows it, and otherwise A's latch is granted only after Y initialises the page, so the variant is case D. The fixture records which branch occurred: A's latch landed on `r`'s frame before Y initialised it, or the allocation path held the frame's latch when the hook paused it. A churn variant has a hook run one CreateTree of another tree to completion between A's read and its re-read, on each of A's attempts; A validates on its first attempt and finishes within the step budget. An exploration runs A, in both modes, with DropTree(X) and a split of Y that can take `r`, under shuttle with no hooks. The property is checked only when A's validation succeeds: the oracle says `r` is mapped and is X's root and X's entry is present, and A never reads or mutates a page the oracle assigns to another tree. Between its latch and its re-read A reads nothing of the page but its header `id`, no case but case E raises XX001, and no case poisons the store. Case E: X's entry stays unchanged, in the normal mode with X `live` and in the test-only mode with X `dropping`, and the fixture clears `r`'s map entry; A's pin finds `r` unmapped, its re-read finds the entry unchanged, and A raises XX001 within the step budget. ROOT-NAME-UNVALIDATED (no re-read under the root's latch) fails case A; ROOT-UNPUBLISH-LATE (DropTree removes the root-table entry after releasing the root's latch) fails case B, where A validates `r` as X's root after Y took it; ROOT-PIN-UNMAPPED-FATAL (a pin of an unmapped LPID on the root-name path raises XX001 without re-reading the entry) fails case C; ROOT-DECODE-BEFORE-VALIDATE (the thread reads the latched page as X's root before the re-read) fails case D; ROOT-HEADER-MISMATCH-FATAL (a header `id` mismatch before the re-read raises XX001) fails case D's second variant where A latches the frame before Y initialises it, and is equivalent on a path whose fixture record shows the allocation path held the frame's latch at the pause, so an equivalent verdict at A-7b's gate rests on that record; ROOT-TABLE-GLOBAL-GEN (validation compares a table-wide counter that every directory change bumps) fails the churn variant, where A retries without bound; ROOT-PIN-UNMAPPED-SPIN (a pin that finds the LPID unmapped retries from (1) even when the re-read finds the entry unchanged) fails case E, where A retries without bound | A-4a (`t_split_reserve_order`, `t_alloc_restart_progress`), A-4b, A-5 (`t_replace_free_shape`), A-7b, B-4d, B-6, A-7d, A-8, A-8a | DROP-ENTRY-SPLIT, DROP-UNLINK-SPLIT, CREATE-TREE-SPLIT, DROP-RESTART-MID-OP, DROP-FREE-BEFORE-REMOVE, DROP-LEAF-CHAINS, DROP-SECOND-WALK, ROOT-NAME-KEPT, CHAIN-BUILD-SPLIT, CHAIN-FREE-SPLIT, CHAIN-NEXT-MAP-SPLIT, CHAIN-NEXT-FREE-SPLIT, LOAD-PAGE-SPLIT, REPLAY-DROP-WALK, DROP-RESTART-AFTER-REMOVE, UNLINK-RESTART-AFTER-UNLINK, SPLIT-RESERVE-AFTER-PRUNE, ALLOC-READ-UNDER-P, DROP-LAST-STEP-PACKS, SPLIT-RESERVE-ALWAYS, REPLACE-FREE-UNDER-P, DELETE-FREE-UNDER-P, CHAIN-BUILD-UNDER-P, ALLOC-RETRY-LOWEST, ALLOC-NEW-MAPLEAF-IN-PLACE, MAPLEAF-CREATE-UNCONDITIONAL, MAPLEAF-FRAME-UNDER-INNER, ROOT-NAME-UNVALIDATED, ROOT-UNPUBLISH-LATE, ROOT-PIN-UNMAPPED-FATAL, ROOT-DECODE-BEFORE-VALIDATE, ROOT-TABLE-GLOBAL-GEN, ROOT-HEADER-MISMATCH-FATAL, ROOT-PIN-UNMAPPED-SPIN |
| I-POOL-BOUND | §5.1/§5.5: the pool never grows; a dirty set of 4× the pool completes | A-3b/A-3c (E4 shape) | POOL-GROW, EVICT-SKIP-DIRTY |

| I-META-SLOT | §2.2, §2.4: the opened meta is a valid copy in a place of its own parity (slot `1 + (ckpt_id mod 2)` or that parity's replica block); a misplaced valid copy, one in a place of the other parity, newer than every valid one is XX001, never opened and never fallen back past | A-2 (`t_meta_copies`' table form), A-7d (SimEnv misdirected meta copy, the meta's other copy lost) | META-WRONG-SLOT |
| I-FREE-SAFE | §4.4/§13 step 6: no reuse before the freeing meta is synced | A-3c, A-7c | FREE-EARLY |
| I-FREE-EXACT | §4.3: the persisted bitmaps are exact | A-7b, A-8 | BITMAP-OMITS-FREESET-PAGES |
| I-META-ATOMIC | §13: a meta never points at unwritten pages | A-7c (crash between every step) | META-BEFORE-SYNC |
| I-CKPT-AT-T | §13: the image is a consistent cut; (a)–(c), with `T` = the apply frontier `M`; (b) bounded by `wal_ckpt_end`/`ts_ckpt_hi`, bulk-load entries via their fence commit; only step 2 writes the image map, from the image-record list. Test `t_epoch_reserve_two_exhausters` (A-3c on stand-ins, with A-4c's shuttle harness; A-7a through `write_guard`; §5.6, §15.1): two first touches exhaust the reserve together; both copies reach the image. In its cross variant two writers each hold a MapLeaf's latch in `write_guard`, A and B, and exhaust the reserve together, every claimable copy being of a page that the other's MapLeaf maps; both finish, both copies reach the image, and a hook asserts that no claim takes a map latch. EXHAUST-DOUBLE-EVICT and EXHAUST-BLOCKING-CLAIM fail it; CLAIM-LATCHES-MAP (a claim records its copy by latching the MapLeaf that maps it) fails the cross variant through the hook, and deadlocks it. Test `t_step2_waits_for_claimed_copy` (A-3c on stand-ins S1–S3 with the read-back S2; A-7d in full; §13 step 2; §15.1): a hook pauses a writer that has claimed an epoch-`k` copy (§5.6 "Reserve") after the copy's write and before its image-record append, just as step 2's data phase reaches its end. Step 2 must wait, and image `k` must hold the copy's content (amcheck, and a read at `T` after a crash at post-5). A slot variant forces slot allocations from a pool at its minimum after the claim's append and before the map phase runs. A map-copy variant evicts in epoch `k+1` a page that MapLeaf M maps, so that M is copied, and has a claim evict M's epoch copy before step 2's data phase writes another page that M maps. A current-home variant has a §5.2 previous-epoch eviction write MapLeaf M, dirty at the flip with no dirty page it maps, to slot `s1`, which becomes M's current home; after the map phase has rewritten M, slot allocations from a pool at its minimum reach `s1`, M is dropped and reloaded through the current map, and amcheck checks image `k`'s slot bitmap. A victim variant pauses a §5.2 previous-epoch victim after its write and before its image-record append, so that step 2's data phase finds the victim latched by its evictor and leaves it to that evictor (§5.2) as the data phase ends. A source variant pauses the map phase after it finds no epoch copy of MapLeaf M and before it latches M's frame, while a writer first-touches M, which installs M's epoch copy, and a claim evicts that copy. A namespace variant lists, in one checkpoint, a record for LPID `n` and one for the MapLeaf at leaf-level position `n` (a claimed copy of it), and adds a radix level in epoch `k+1` before the map phase runs (I-MAP-GROW, §4.1 "Growth"). An adoption variant pauses step 2's data phase after it picks a dirty page P and before it latches P's frame, while a §5.2 previous-epoch eviction writes and lists P and P is reloaded; a hook asserts that the list holds at most one record per page and that no adoption replaces a home the list names. An allocation variant runs a checkpoint with a hook that fails any allocation the image-record list or a map-phase buffer makes after open; the checkpoint completes. A stale-claimant variant: a claim C chooses MapLeaf M's epoch copy, in frame `f`, as its candidate, and a hook pauses it before its `try_latch`; the map phase then takes and detaches the copy. In a detach form C resumes immediately after the map phase has released `f`'s latch, with the map phase held at that point and so before it returns `f` to the reserve; C wins `f`'s latch, its re-check finds `f` no longer M's epoch copy, and it moves on. In a draw form C resumes after `f` is back in the reserve, wins `f`'s latch and is paused before its re-check, with `f` the only free reserve frame, the epoch copy of a map page the map phase has not reached left to claim, and the map phase held by a hook after it returned `f`; a writer holding a page latch first-touches another map page the map phase has not reached, passes over `f`, claims that copy and finishes, and only then does C re-check and move on. A hook asserts that no draw waits on a frame latch, and no record for M is appended after the map phase took M's copy. STEP2-NO-WAIT (step 2's data phase ends with a claimed copy outstanding) and IMAGE-RECORD-AFTER-RELEASE (a claim appends its record after it releases the copy's latch) fail it: image `k` misses the page's epoch-`k` content. IMAGE-SLOT-REUSED (a slot that only the list names is reusable before the map phase applies it) fails the slot variant: a later write lands on the listed slot, and amcheck finds the page's CRC wrong. MAP-PHASE-DROPS-CLAIMED-COPY (the map phase builds M from its frame although the list records M's claimed copy) fails the map-copy variant: image `k`'s M maps the evicted page's epoch-`k+1` home, which a read at `T` after a crash at post-5 returns; MAP-HOME-SLOT-REUSED (a slot the list records for a map page becomes reusable once the map phase rewrites the page, although it is the page's current home) fails the current-home variant: the reload reads a reused slot and fails its CRC check with XX001; MAP-PHASE-SKIPS-VICTIM (the map phase starts while a §5.2 previous-epoch victim's write is in flight) fails the victim variant: image `k` maps the victim's earlier home, which a read at `T` after a crash at post-5 returns; MAP-SOURCE-UNCHECKED (the map phase builds a map page from its frame without checking the frame's content epoch under its latch) fails the source variant: image `k`'s M holds epoch-`k+1` entries, which a read at `T` after a crash at post-5 follows; MAP-POSITION-AS-LPID (a map page's record is keyed as an LPID) fails the namespace variant: one record replaces the other, and amcheck finds image `k`'s map wrong; ADOPT-LISTED-HOME (the data phase decides a page's write and its adoption before it latches the frame) fails the adoption variant through the hook; IMAGE-LIST-GROWS (the image-record list or a map-phase buffer allocates during a checkpoint) fails the allocation variant: the failed allocation is fail-stop with 53200; MAP-COPY-NOT-DETACHED (the map phase releases a map page's epoch copy's latch before it detaches the copy, so the frame is still claimable as that copy) fails the detach form: C's re-check passes and it appends a record for M after the map phase took M's copy, which the hook and §13's debug assertion catch; RESERVE-DRAW-BLOCKS (`write_guard` draws a reserve frame with a blocking latch acquire) fails the draw form through the hook: the writer waits on C's latch while it holds its page latch | A-3c (the reserve, the image-record list and the map phase; `t_step2_waits_for_claimed_copy` (stand-ins; full form A-7d)), A-7c (splits and packs during a checkpoint; async mix; S2, S3), A-7d (the crash forms, FLIP-NO-GATE's splits and packs across a flip among them), B-1c (CKPT-T-VISIBLE with the real frontier), B-1d (a checkpoint during a finalize) | FLIP-NO-GATE, WAL-SYNC-T-ONLY, CKPT-T-VISIBLE, EXHAUST-DOUBLE-EVICT, EXHAUST-BLOCKING-CLAIM, STEP2-NO-WAIT, CLAIM-LATCHES-MAP, IMAGE-RECORD-AFTER-RELEASE, IMAGE-SLOT-REUSED, MAP-PHASE-DROPS-CLAIMED-COPY, MAP-HOME-SLOT-REUSED, MAP-PHASE-SKIPS-VICTIM, MAP-SOURCE-UNCHECKED, MAP-POSITION-AS-LPID, ADOPT-LISTED-HOME, IMAGE-LIST-GROWS, MAP-COPY-NOT-DETACHED, RESERVE-DRAW-BLOCKS |

| I-WAL-COVER | §13 step 0, §14 step 3a: meta `k` records `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`. An open fails with XX001 before pass 2 and writes nothing if its valid end (the walk's stop position, §12.5) is below `wal_ckpt_end`, or if its `Commit`/`CommitMarker` records do not reach `ts_ckpt_hi`. No open holds an entry above its recovered frontier. Test `t_wal_loss_below_ckpt_end`: a SimEnv fixture with post-sync WAL faults (LOST, TORN or MISDIRECTED on a synced WAL write) holds a commit `c > T` partly applied before the flip, whose record step 1 synced; then meta `k` is durable, then a fault on `c`'s record. The open gives XX001 and every file is byte-identical afterwards. Variants: the fault on a record that was appended but unapplied at the flip; on a `SegmentEnd` before `wal_ckpt_end`; on a chunk of a marked transaction; on a chunk of a transaction that never committed (no committed data lost; still XX001); on a bulk load's fence commit `f > T`, with loaded entries in image `k` (a stand-in load at A-7d; B-4d's load at B-4d, §15.1); **short segment**: a segment cut short so that its end-of-file lies past the start of a record that runs past it, below `wal_ckpt_end`. Under COVER-CHECK-SKIP, a new commit and then a read at `S ≥ c` see part of `c`; under COVER-END-AT-T, the open succeeds; under COVER-AFTER-TRUNCATE, the files differ; under COVER-AT-EOF, the short-segment variant opens. Test `t_cover_ts_cross_check`: a meta whose `ts_ckpt_hi` exceeds every replayable commit (forged through `Env`) gives XX001; for §14 step 1's open-time check, a forged meta with `T > ts_ckpt_hi`, and one with `wal_start_lsn > wal_ckpt_end`, each with a valid CRC, give XX001 (META-RANGE-SKIP opens them) | A-7c (the fields), A-7d (SimEnv post-sync WAL damage), B-4d (the bulk-load variant's real load; §15.1) | COVER-CHECK-SKIP, COVER-END-AT-T, COVER-AFTER-TRUNCATE, TS-HI-CHECK-SKIP, COVER-AT-EOF, META-RANGE-SKIP |
| I-WAL-CHAIN | §2.2, §12.2, §12.5, §14 step 3a: every record carries `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`, the start and the `crc` of the record appended before it (`SegmentEnd` included); a boot's first record names its recovery's last accepted record, or the opened meta's `wal_start_prev` and `wal_start_prev_crc`. The chain is checked last, after every stop check, and both fields are compared. A record that passes every stop check but names another predecessor (either field differs) is a splice, never an end: a read-write open fails with XX001 before pass 2 and writes nothing, and a read-only open serves salvage cut before it (§14.1). The stop checks keep their meaning. Undetected only when the spliced-in record's predecessor is byte-identical, at the same position, to the walk's (or their CRCs collide). | I-RO-OPEN | §14.1: a read-only open writes nothing through `Env`, and its reads at every registered `S` equal those of a read-write open of the same files after step 4, except under WAL loss or a splice (§14 step 3a), a missing start segment (step 3), an unmet checkpoint dependency (step 3b) or an unproven fall-back (step 4a), where the read-write open fails and the read-only open serves the salvage state at the recovered frontier (§14.1). Every build offers it (§14.1). Test `t_ro_open`, with a SimEnv that fails every write, sync, rename and truncate. Its fixture is a crashed file with a torn WAL tail, small commits above `T`, a marked chunked txn whose marker lies after a commit `≤ T`, unmarked chunks, a `dropping` entry, a replayed `DropTree`, provisional entries in the image, and replay splits. The test compares the reads with a read-write open of a copy, checks the files are byte-identical after close, checks that a pool too small for the replay gives 53200 with nothing written, and checks that the replayed `DropTree`'s tree keeps every page mapped (REPLAY-DROP-WALK fails it). Test `t_ro_open_disk_full` (the stopped store, C4 decision 5; A-7d with the walk scan S6, B-6 with A-4e's cursor; §15.1): a SimEnv stops a writer with ENOSPC (53100) at each write point in turn, namely a WAL append, a segment's creation, its preallocation (a short segment, §12.5), a file extension (§4.5), a checkpoint page write and the meta write. A read-only open then runs on a SimEnv that reports zero free bytes and refuses every write, including a lock file's creation. It must succeed, its reads must equal those of a read-write open of a copy on a roomy Env, a full scan of every published tree must succeed, and every file must be byte-identical after close. With a pool below the replay's evictable minimum it must give 53200 with nothing written, and a read-only open of the same files with a larger pool budget must then succeed at zero free bytes (§14.1's remedy). SHORT-SEG-ERROR (a short or missing segment returns 58030 instead of ending the valid end) fails the open. Test `t_seg_ack_durable` (A-6b on the walk; A-7d with acks and replay; §12.5's writer rule; §15.1), with a SimEnv whose power cut also loses unsynced creates, renames and `set_len`: a segment is newly created (the lost-create case) or recycled by rename (the lost-rename case), records in it are acked, and power is cut at every step from the create or rename to the ack. Recovery must replay every acked record. SEG-ACK-BEFORE-DURABLE (ack before the segment's `sync_all` and its `wal/` directory sync) loses an acked commit as a clean stop and fails it. Test `t_wal_loss_salvage` (Q1 = A, PLAN-v3 §10 item 8; A-7d with the directory as the catalog's tree list (S3) and the walk scan S6; B-6 with the catalog and A-4e's cursor; §15.1): the `t_wal_loss_below_ckpt_end` fixture, plus a `dropping` mark from a drop-cleanup commit above `T` whose record is lost, opened read-only. At every admitted `S` it reads the model's state at `S ≤` the frontier, never an entry above it, and a full scan of every tree the catalog at `S` names succeeds. SALVAGE-ABOVE-FRONTIER fails it. amcheck (A-8) checks I-TREE, I-ENC-ROUNDTRIP, I-TAG-RESERVED, I-LPID-EXACT, I-FREE-EXACT and I-INTEGRITY on a quiescent file. At open (§14 step 7) it runs before the boot job's walks start, or holds the gate exclusively, because a running walk is not quiescent: either way it reads a closed graph. A crash image's `dropping` tree gets no exemption (I-LINK-ATOMIC).

### 15.1 Test ownership

One row per card that runs a test or a form of it: *Needs* names the cards whose code the form uses besides its own, each, for a Phase A card, in that card's Depends closure (PLAN-v3 §9 A); *Form* is full, or the stand-ins it uses (S1–S6) and the variants it runs; *Mutants* are those judged on that card, by name. The Phase B cards in its Card column are B-1a, B-1b, B-1c, B-1d, B-2c, B-3, B-4d, B-6, B-7 and R-1; its Needs column also names B-2b and B-2e. A mutant that no Phase A row judges is pinned only in Phase B (PLAN-v3 §8). Every mutant that draft 7.8 judged on a Phase A card is judged on a Phase A row here, and a change to this table keeps it so. Every mutant added since draft 7.10 is judged on a Phase A row too.

## 16. Measured evidence

All numbers are from n5000 (i5-13500H, 16 CPUs), rustc 1.99.0, in memory, medians of 3 runs, unless noted. Workload sources: `spikes/e1-bench/README.md` at 7ccd1cff.
- **Commits measured at:** W1 (b)/(c), W2, W3 and W4 at d28582e8; W1 (a), W5 and W6 at c39656fd.
- **Gate re-measurements:** `verdicts/X-1b.md` (rounds 1 and 2) and `verdicts/X-2.md`.
- **Decision:** PLAN-v3 §4, "E1′ decision".

| Design element here | Measured | Source |
|---|---|---|
| N3 = (b) (§7) | W1 CPU/txn (b) vs (c): 4.0/3.8 µs (t=1), 9.4/7.9 (t=4), 15.7/19.5 (t=8), 69.1/117.0 (t=16): never ≥ 2× for (c). W2 reader throughput (c)/(b): 1.18× (0 writers), 1.38× (10), 1.12× (100) | README §W1, §W2; decision block, rule clauses 2–3 and ruling R1 |
| One-seek point read (§8.1) | W2 reads at 0 writers: (b) 766,882 ops/s, p50 9,058 ns | README §W2 |
| Escape cost (§7.4) | 124.07 B applied per W1 txn (100 B values), of which 13.07 B is the escaped 8-byte key | README §W1; `e1-bench/src/util.rs:67` |
| Version skipping in scans (§8.3) | W3 100-row scans at 10 versions/key: latest (b) 88,019 ns vs (c) 10,715 ns (8.2×); old snapshot (b) 87,627 ns vs (c) 146,156 ns | README §W3; decision block "Known costs" |
| Merge and free set (§6.5, §11) | W4 (b) tree/logical 20.11 → 20.11 after GC, although versions fell 1.1M → 100k (1.13 MB freed): E0 never merges or frees | README §W4; `e0-btree/src/tree.rs` T-NOMERGE; decision block |
| Tombstone elision rule (§10.2) | The spike's `above_w == 0` elision leaves extra tombstones and inflates (b)'s space; X-2-F1 rule adopted | `verdicts/X-2.md` finding 1; decision block "Consequences" |
| Pool and dirty eviction govern retention (§5.5) | W5 long reader: (b) retained 3,000,000 versions, peak tree 536,436,736 B, inside a preallocated 1 GiB pool; VmHWM 1,874 MiB is pool-dominated, not per-consumer evidence | README §W5; `verdicts/X-1b.md`; decision block ruling R2, assumptions 1–2 |
| Eviction exercised only once so far | X-2 gate: S13 at 64 frames never exceeds 1 page; s14 at 16 frames reaches 854 pages / 100,000 versions | `verdicts/X-2.md` (experiment evidence, finding 2 → X-2-F2) |
| Eviction cost of the simple pool (§5) | E0 at a 1/4 pool: point 2,201 vs 1,182 ns in memory (1.9×); insert 2,745 vs 1,477 ns | `e0-btree/README.md` RESULT |
| Simple buffer manager is enough (N8) | E0 in memory vs SQLite: point 1,182 vs 1,317 ns (1.11×), range 7,003 vs 10,952 ns (1.56×): PASS; 8-thread W-mt only 1.09× the single-thread rate (the E7 input) | `e0-btree/README.md` RESULT |
| Large-transaction volume (§12.7) | W6 10M-row UPDATE: (b) post-commit tree ≈ 5.32 GB (≈ 649k pages), write set 117 B/key, commit 20.4 s, head-of-line p99 (= max) 17.94 s | README §W6; `verdicts/X-1b.md` |

## 17. Boundaries

**C-T0 v8 (card S-2) owns, and this file does not specify:**
- **Watermark.** The computation, publication and monotonicity of `W`, and its floor after a restart (`≥` meta `gc_w`, §10.5). That `W ≤` every registered snapshot and `W ≤ M`, the apply frontier (hence `W ≤ visible_ts`), is the precondition this file relies on (§10.1, §10.5).
- **The write set and lock table:** own-write merge (I-HALLOWEEN), escalation, NOWAIT/SKIP LOCKED.
- **The write-side rules:** the RR check after the lock wait, unique checks and ON CONFLICT, using §8.2.
- **Commit-visibility semantics.** The commit ack waits for `visible_ts ≥ c` (decision block, "Commit visibility"). There is no session floor: C-T0 v8 §3.4 rejects it and says why.
- **Pre-applying large transactions** as transaction-tagged provisional entries (decision block, "Large-txn head-of-line"). Specified in C-T0 v8 §2.4 and §3.2. This file holds the encoding (§7.1, §7.3 tags 6–8), the read rule (§8), chunk supersede (§12.7, §14) and the image rule (I-CKPT-AT-T (c)), from the S-2 co-edit.
- **Lock release at commit cheaper than O(keys)** (325k–437k probes slipped through the release tail in W6): C-T0 v8 §3.3.
- **Unrelated commits stalled** behind a big apply by the ts-ordered `visible_ts` advance: C-T0 v8 §3.1–§3.2.
- **SSI** without intents.
- **GC policy:** when sweeps run, their budgets, and DDL retirement timing (C-T0 v7.8 §9.2–9.3).
- **Bounds on disk growth under long readers.** Whether on-disk version growth under long readers needs a bound is S-2/B-3's call (decision block, ruling R2 "Not R-1's call"). This file only guarantees that the growth costs disk, not RAM (§5.5).

**S-3 owns:** the row format, TOAST semantics over §6.7's chains, and the online index build's steps under the §12.8 rule. That includes registering a snapshot at `R` **before** picking the load's `S > R` and keeping it registered for the whole load (§10.1), and how an unfinished build is restarted. S-3 should also record §10.1's reasoning for why this condition has no mutant.

**R-1 owns:** the numeric budgets per mode, `F`, `R_EPOCH`, and the per-consumer accounting of §5.7.

**D-6 and D-8 own:** backup, PITR and replica handling of the §12.8 boundary; format versioning and upgrades.

## 18. Refinements of PLAN-v3 §3.3

This file wins over the plan (precedence above). The plan should be updated through an Opus card to match these. R10 refines the §4 decision block rather than §3.3. R11 comes from the S-2 co-edit. R12–R15 come from card S-1-F3, R16 from card S-1-F5, R17 from card S-1-F7, and R18–R22 from card S-ALPHA; R15 refines §3.4, as R9 does:

| # | Plan text | Here | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Meta fields: "checkpoint id, T, the page-map root, the free-set root, the catalog root, the WAL start LSN and a CRC" | Adds `gc_w`, `epoch`, `next_lpid`, `slot_count`; the root fields are refs with epoch and CRC; "catalog root" is the tree directory's root (§2.2) | `gc_w` replaces v7.8's synced `/sys/gc_w` (§10.5). The others are needed by I-FREE-EXACT and I-INTEGRITY on physically addressed pages |
| R2 | Step 1: "WAL `sync_data` through the last record with commit_ts ≤ T" | Through the end of every record appended before the flip (§13 step 1) | Applies finish out of ts order (`commit.rs`), so the image can hold part of a commit `> T` |
| R3 | Step 0: "Pause commit-apply briefly" | The epoch gate, held by every gated operation (§5.6) | A split, pack, GC or eviction that straddled the flip would tear the image (I-CKPT-AT-T) |
| R4 | Bulk loads: "S-1 picks one" | Force a checkpoint before publication (§12.8) | Versions at a registered ts `≤ T` cannot be placed by a replay filtered on `commit_ts > T`; page images carry LPIDs |
| R5 | B+tree (no root rule) | Stable root LPIDs (§6.3) | The directory and images never change on root splits |
| R6 | Free set (no representation) | Two bitmaps, slots and LPIDs (§4.3) | Memory is a fixed fraction of the file; no fixed-point problem at serialisation |
| R7 | WAL order (unstated) | I-WAL-TS-ORDER (§12.4) | Required for the ts-prefix that E4's oracle checks |
| R8 | "split and merge" (no merge rule) | The pack step with `F_PACK = 0.85`, driven by the GC sweep (§6.5, §10.4); I-SPACE (§11) | The decision block requires GC to return space; B-3's 1.5× acceptance |
| R9 | §3.4 GC "prunes versions below the watermark during page writes and splits" | Page-local prune (required at splits, optional at eviction) **plus** a whole-tree sweep (§10.3–10.4) | Page-local pruning cannot see groups that span leaves, so alone it cannot meet I-GC-COMPLETE or B-3's bound |
| R10 | §4 decision block, "Space amplification": "after-GC tree bytes" (undefined) | Tree bytes = reachable pages × `page_size` (§11) | The file never shrinks (§4.5), so a file-length or allocated-page reading stays at the post-update peak and B-3 could never pass |
| R11 | Step 0: "T = the apply frontier (every commit ≤ T is fully applied to the trees; under N2, trees hold only committed data)" | `T` = C-T0 v8's `M`; trees may also hold provisional entries, which are never data after recovery (§1, §13 (c)) | X-4 fired the pre-apply trigger; C-T0 v8 §3.2 |
| R12 | Meta fields (R1's list) | Adds `wal_ckpt_end`, `ts_ckpt_hi`, `boot_id` (§2.2) | I-WAL-COVER and the ordered boot-id stop |
| R13 | Recovery: "walk … to the valid end", with no rule for an early end | The coverage check against the walk's stop position: fail closed with XX001, write nothing, and a read-only salvage that every build offers (§14 step 3a, §14.1; PLAN-v3 §10 item 8) | E4 class MW (X-5c-F3) |
| R14 | Group commit: "one writer … then acks the group" | I-ACK-PREFIX (§12.4) | X-5c-F3 seed 21 |
| R15 | §3.4's fail-stop (I/O error, disk full) | I-FAIL-STOP includes XX001 inside gated operations, checkpoints and store jobs, and 58030/53100/53200/XX001 on the WAL path; client reads never poison; every wait, the lock-table waits included, observes the poison (§9.2) | G8, MP-LIVELOCK 41 → 0 |
| R16 | WAL record framing (unstated) and meta fields (R12) | Records carry `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`, the meta carries `wal_start_prev` and `wal_start_prev_crc`, and the walk checks the chain (I-WAL-CHAIN, §12.5, §14 step 3a) | Owner decision, PLAN-v3 §10 item 9 (2026-11-03): back-pointers in the 2.0 format, because adding them later needs a format migration. Its addendum (owner, 2026-11-03): the chain also carries the predecessor's CRC, as SQLite's does, because a position alone misses two boots appending equal-length records from the same point |
| R17 | "DropTree frees a tree's pages bottom-up, and an unfinished drop resumes at open" | The entry removal and the root free are one gated operation, entry first; a chain-bearing entry goes with its chain in its own gated operation; an operation restarts only before its first mutation; an LPID is mapped and unmapped in the same gated operation as its reference (I-LINK-ATOMIC, §5.6, §6.8); and pass 2 runs no walk (§14 step 4) | E4's second design finding: a flip between the root free and the entry removal gave a refused open, a replay that freed another tree's live page, or an image whose dropping entry named one (28 of 51 C-XX rounds). Page headers carry no tree id (§3.1), so a recovery arm cannot tell a reused root from the tree's own |
| R18 | "Slots 1–2 = two meta slots: meta `k` goes to slot `1 + (k mod 2)`, so a torn meta write can destroy only the older one"; "At open, the meta with the highest checkpoint id whose magic and CRC are valid and which sits in its own slot wins. There is no fallback to the older meta" | Every meta is written twice, to its slot and to a replica block at a fixed byte offset, each copy with a `copy` byte and the file's static fields; the open selects among four places by §2.2's table, with a tie rule and the misplaced rule; a newest image that fails at open falls back one generation with proof that the log is complete, and otherwise the read-write open is refused and an explicit rollback can open it (§2.2, §2.4, §14 step 4a; I-META-COPIES, I-FALLBACK) | Owner decision, PLAN-v3 §10 item 16 (2026-11-10): "two places (copies differ in bytes, tie rule), two generations; automatic fall-back needs proof the log is complete, else refuse + explicit rollback" |
| R19 | Step 6: "Only now free the previous image's superseded slots, and recycle the WAL segments wholly below the start LSN"; "slots freed by image k may be reused only after meta k is synced (I-FREE-SAFE)"; "Segments wholly below the durable meta's start LSN may be recycled" | One checkpoint later: step 6 of checkpoint `k` frees image `k−2`'s superseded slots and recycles below meta `k−1`'s start, lowest first, each rename durable before the next; an open protects the previous image (§4.4, §12.1, §12.6, §13 step 6; I-TWO-GEN, I-WAL-START) | The same decision's "two generations": a fall-back of one generation is sound only if that image and its WAL span are intact |
| R20 | Bulk loads [R4]: "The publishing commit may be issued only after the forced checkpoint's meta is durable" | The publishing commit also names that checkpoint (`RequireCkpt`), and recovery refuses to replay it over an older image (§12.3, §12.8, §14 step 3b; I-CKPT-DEP) | E4's first silent-loss path (`header-design-verify.md` §1.1–§1.3), fixed in the prototype by card E4-SILENT-1; PLAN-v3 §10 item 16 (3): "dependency names the checkpoint id, not a log position" |
| R21 | File creation, and recovery's walk: "A segment cut short or missing also ends it" | Segment 0 exists from creation; every meta's start segment exists before the meta is written; a missing start segment is XX001, never an empty WAL (§2.3, §12.5, §13 step 1, §14 step 3; I-WAL-START) | E4's second silent-loss path (`header-design-verify.md` §1.4), fixed in the prototype by card E4-SILENT-1 |
| R22 | Page map: "a radix tree of physically addressed pages whose inner pages stay resident" (no growth rule) | The root is a MapInner whose level the meta records; a new level or MapInner is made in an allocation restart's own gated operation, under the current root's or the parent's exclusive latch, only if still missing (§4.1 "Growth"; I-MAP-GROW) | Verdict S-1-F7-F2-F2 at d909ebba, anomaly (a) and follow-up S-1-F7-F2-F2-F1: map growth was unspecified, and two restarts making one MapInner or level could lose a mapping |

## 19. Open questions, known costs, revisit triggers

**Open:**
1. **File shrinking.** GC returns space to the free set, not to the filesystem (§4.5). Trigger for a tail-truncation step (at step 6, of free tail slots only) or an offline compaction: a browser quota (E6/D-5) or a user report of a file that does not shrink after a mass delete.
2. **Leaf prefix compression within groups.** Every version repeats its full escaped key, so a 10-version group of W1-sized keys spends about 117 B on repeated key bytes. A-4d covers inner nodes only. Trigger: NBench space rows, or I-SPACE met but B-3's bytes measured close to 1.5×.
3. **Optimistic latches and pointer swizzling** (N8/N9): only on the E0/E7 triggers (PLAN-v3 §3.12).
4. ~~**Provisional entries.**~~ Closed by the S-2 co-edit: §7.1, §7.3 tags 6–8, §8, §12.7, I-CKPT-AT-T (c), §14 (C-T0 v8 §2.4).
5. **PITR and replicas across a §12.8 bulk load** (D-6).
6. **Resumable overflow-chain free or build** if A-5 measures the flip hold of a gated operation as too long (§6.7): a chain's build or free, a replace, a GC sweep's or DropTree walk's chain delete over cold chains (§6.8, §10.4). A resumable chain free or build must keep I-LINK-ATOMIC (§5.6; mutants CHAIN-NEXT-MAP-SPLIT, CHAIN-NEXT-FREE-SPLIT).

**Known costs of (b), accepted, with revisit triggers (from the decision block, restated so the storage cards carry them):**
- **T1: latest-snapshot scans.** 8.2× slower than (c) at 10 versions/key (W3). Mitigations: GC at the watermark (B-3) and version skipping (§8.3, A-4e/B-3). **Trigger:** after both mitigations, the NBench OLTP range rows or E9 miss target, and a profile attributes more than 20% of scan CPU to stepping over invisible versions. Then an Opus card re-opens N3.
- **T2: space amplification after GC.** 20.11× in E0. **Trigger:** B-3's W3/W4-shape acceptance (after-GC tree bytes `≤ 1.5×` pre-update, both as reachable pages × `page_size`, §11) fails on the real tree. Then N3 is re-opened.
- **T3: contended writes.** Both arms collapse past 8 threads. This is a shared commit-path or latching bottleneck owned by S-2 and E7, not by N3 or this file.

**Known cost of a single-copy WAL (cards S-1-F3, S-1-F5):**
- **T4: undetectable post-sync WAL loss** (§14, "Post-sync damage").
  - **The cost:** acked commits since the last checkpoint can be lost silently on a device that drops synced writes: a lost tail, or damage the walk stops at, above `wal_ckpt_end`.
  - **2.0 has WAL back-pointers** (I-WAL-CHAIN: `prev_lsn` and `prev_crc`; owner decision, PLAN-v3 §10 item 9, and its addendum adding `prev_crc`, both 2026-11-03). They catch a mid-log splice unless the spliced-in record's predecessor is byte-identical at the same position, and they fail it closed, but they catch neither a lost tail nor damage the walk stops at, and they add no recovery (§12.5).
  - **Hardware guidance (for D-9's release notes and the operator docs).** Durability of acked commits since the last checkpoint assumes a device that honours flush/FUA:
    - enterprise SSDs with **power-loss protection**, or consumer drives whose volatile write cache honours flush;
    - not virtual disks or network block devices configured to ignore flushes (for example, a hypervisor `cache=unsafe`).

    On browser OPFS, it is whatever the browser's flush guarantees (E6).
  - **Roadmap: opt-in synchronous replication for server mode** (PLAN-v3 §10 item 9). An `on` commit is acked only once a replica on another machine also holds its record. This is the real durability guarantee against a device that lies, because it gives both detection and repair. It builds on D-6's WAL-streaming replicas, which carry the chain (`prev_lsn`, `prev_crc`) through. A second WAL copy on the same device barely helps, because the same device can lie for both.
  - **Other mitigations, not in 2.0:** per-batch `durable_end` witnesses with a forward scan past the stop (detection only); more frequent checkpoints (a narrower window).
  - **Two generations change one case** (card S-ALPHA). When a device drops the sync that covered both copies of the newest meta, the open selects the previous meta, whose image and WAL span are intact (I-TWO-GEN), and serves the state its WAL reaches: acked commits lost with the dropped WAL bytes stay lost without detection, as above, where draft 7.10 often refused instead, because the older image's slots had been reused. The owner adopted this with the header design (PLAN-v3 §10 item 16; `header-durability-design-eval.md` §7, "Outcome under F2").
  - **Trigger:** a field report on a target that drops synced writes, a splice XX001 reported from the field, or server mode's synchronous-replication card.

**Known cost of root names (cards S-1-F7, S-1-F7-F2):**
- **T5: no root name kept across operations.** §6.8's root names let no thread keep a tree's root LPID across operations: every operation names its root through the root table and validates it under the root's latch.
  - **The cost.** One root-table read and one re-read per operation, in place of draft 7.5's two-latch directory descent (about +240 ns per point get, and 1.46× at 4 threads to 2.08× at 16, on E0's tree, verdict S-1-F7). No ordinary operation latches the directory, so that global cache line leaves the hot path, provided a root-name read stores to no memory common to all trees (§6.8 "The root table"). Each operation still takes its tree's root latch, a read-modify-write on a cache line per tree, so a workload on one hot table keeps E0's scaling cliff; the remedy is the E0/E7 optimistic-latch trigger (PLAN-v3 §3.12), not this rule.
  - **Measurement.** A-5, with E0, measures the table path: the point-op CPU spent naming roots, and multi-tree scaling at 4 to 16 threads. ROOT-TABLE-SHARED-WRITE (a root-name read takes a read guard or a reference count on memory common to all trees) shows there, not in a functional test.
  - **Later.** C-T6 §7.5 keeps a tree from being dropped while a registered snapshot can see it, so a foreground operation could keep a root name for its registered snapshot. That is a later spec edit, opened if A-5's measurement puts the table path above 5% of point-op CPU; background jobs keep the current rule.

**Known cost of the unpacked directory (card S-1-F7-F2-F3):**
- **T6: directory pages are never reclaimed.** No directory page is packed or freed in 2.0 (§6.8 step 3), so the directory's size follows the trees ever created, not the trees that exist.
  - **The cost.** On the order of one leaf page per hundred trees ever created, at 8 KiB pages, kept for the store's life. DROP, TRUNCATE and a table rewrite each retire tree ids (C-T0 v8 §9.3), so a workload that repeats them grows the directory with no growth in live trees. The open's root-table build (§14 step 2) reads every directory leaf, the empty ones included.
  - **Trigger.** A directory that takes more than 1% of the file under a TRUNCATE- or rewrite-heavy workload, or a root-table build that dominates open time. Then a card defines how directory pages are reclaimed, which must keep I-LATCH and §5.4's quota, the constraints that stop DropTree's last step from packing (§6.8 step 3).

**Known cost of two generations (card S-ALPHA):**
- **T7: one more checkpoint interval of freed pages and WAL.** Image `k−1`'s superseded slots are reused one checkpoint later (I-FREE-SAFE, I-TWO-GEN), and the WAL from meta `k−1`'s start is kept, so the file's high-water mark and the WAL's size grow by about one checkpoint interval's rewritten pages and log. The file never shrinks (Open 1).
  - **Measured only on E4's prototype:** 173.6 slots per checkpoint on average against 198.3 (+14%), 590 at most against 706 (+20%), and 2.92 WAL segments on average against 3.00 (`header-design-verify.md` §2.4), on a tiny store whose numbers do not carry over.
  - **Measurement:** a space run with two generations on and off, on the E1 workloads and a browser-sized configuration, on the real store (PLAN-v3 §9 A-7e).
  - **Trigger:** a measured cost that a browser quota (E6, D-5) cannot carry. Then the owner decides whether browser mode keeps two generations; the fall-back (I-FALLBACK) needs them wherever it is on.

## Changelog

- **Draft 7.11** (2026-11-15, card S-ALPHA, from the owner's header decision of 2026-11-10 (PLAN-v3 §10 item 16; QUEUE S-HDR-1), with the spec side of card E4-SILENT-1, and from verdict S-1-F7-F2-F2's follow-up S-1-F7-F2-F2-F1 (d909ebba)):
  - **Two places** (§2.1, §2.2, new §2.4, §13 step 4; I-META-COPIES, I-META-SLOT).
    - Every meta is written twice: its primary copy to slot `1 + (k mod 2)`, and its replica to a 4096-byte block at a fixed offset, 1 MiB for even `ckpt_id`s and 1 MiB + 4 KiB for odd ones, both before step 5's sync.
    - The copies differ in a `copy` byte, and each carries page 0's static fields and `creation_uuid`, so an open needs no page 0 and ignores a foreign copy.
    - The open selects among four places by one table. Each parity's valid copy with the higher `ckpt_id` is its candidate; a tie of one `ckpt_id` with other bytes goes to the newer `boot_id`, and equal ones are XX001; a misplaced copy, one in a place of the other parity, fails the open only if no valid copy has an equal or higher `ckpt_id`.
    - The slots under the replica region are reserved, and a file is created at least as long as the region.
  - **Two generations** (§4.4, §12.6, §13 step 6, §14 step 2; I-TWO-GEN, I-FREE-SAFE). Step 6 releases image `k−2`'s superseded slots and recycles below meta `k−1`'s start, one checkpoint later than at draft 7.10, so image `k−1` and its WAL span survive until meta `k+1` is durable. An open protects the previous image's slots from its free set. I-FREE-SAFE now says meta `k+1`.
  - **Fall-back** (§14 step 4a, new §14.2, §14.1; I-FALLBACK). §2.2's "no fallback" is replaced. A newest image that fails an integrity check at open falls back to the previous meta, with every check against it, and automatically only with proof that the walk reaches the failed meta's `wal_ckpt_end` and `ts_ckpt_hi`; a read-write fall-back first zeroes both copies of the abandoned meta. Unproven, the read-write open fails with XX001, a read-only open serves salvage, and an explicit rollback naming the abandoned meta opens it read-write (55000 when its precondition does not hold). §14 step 1's range check keeps its "no fallback".
  - **The two silent-loss fixes** (`header-design-verify.md` §1.1–§1.5; card E4-SILENT-1).
    - A publishing commit carries `RequireCkpt` with the forced checkpoint's `ckpt_id`, and recovery refuses to replay it over an older image (§12.3, §12.8, §14 step 3b; I-CKPT-DEP).
    - Segment 0 is made at file creation, step 1 makes every meta's start segment exist, recycling runs lowest first with each rename durable, and a missing start segment is XX001, never an empty WAL (§2.3, §12.1, §12.5, §13 steps 1 and 6, §14 step 3; I-WAL-START).
    - I-REDO-COMPLETE's "I-WAL-COVER after recycling" did not cover an older meta at a quiescent flip, and an intact image and WAL span did not cover a load: both are corrected. §14's post-sync damage list gains the meta and image cases.
  - **Map growth** (§3.1, §4.1, §5.6, §5.7, §13 steps 0 and 2; I-MAP-GROW). The root is always a MapInner, and the meta records its level. A new level is made under the current root's exclusive latch, and a MapInner under its parent's, each only if still missing, in the allocation restart's own gated operation. Positions are counted from the leaf level, and image `k` is built up to the root step 0 recorded. A test build may lower the fanout. S1 records the map's root and level too.
  - **§15 and §15.1.** Six invariants; six tests (`t_meta_copies`, `t_replica_region`, `t_two_gen`, `t_fallback`, `t_ckpt_dep`, `t_wal_start`) and new variants of `t_alloc_restart_progress` and `t_step2_waits_for_claimed_copy`; thirty-one mutants, each judged on a Phase A row; nineteen §15.1 rows. S3 gains the publishing commit, the growth call and step 6's release. META-WRONG-SLOT is judged at A-2 as well, and RO-WRITES covers a retire write. §15.1's intro adds that every mutant added since draft 7.10 is judged on a Phase A row.
  - §18 gains R18–R22. §19 gains T7 (two generations' space) and a T4 note on the dropped-sync case. The error table gains 55000.
  - **The byte layout changes:** the meta record (`copy`, page 0's static fields, `map_level`), the replica blocks, the `RequireCkpt` op, and map pages' `id`. No invariant is weakened: I-FREE-SAFE and I-REDO-COMPLETE are stated more strictly, I-META-SLOT and I-RO-OPEN cover the new places and refusals, and no existing mutant or test is removed or renamed.
  - **Gate corrections** (`verdicts/S-ALPHA.md`, REWORK): pass-2 evictions made before image `k` fails, and the retire write after them; I-FREE-SAFE's exception for an open with no previous image; no fall-back unless step 2 read the previous free set; §11's reuse one checkpoint later; the fixtures that roll a meta back now lose both copies; the chained fall-back after two lost metas, stated; the retire write zeroes a misplaced copy first; the identity rule's order; and the test texts that a correct build could not pass, or whose mutant could not fail.
- **Draft 7.10** (2026-11-15, card S-15-STANDINS-2, from S-15-STANDINS-F1 and -F2, `verdicts/S-15-STANDINS.md`):
  - **Where mutants are judged** (§15.1):
    - GCW-BOUND-SKIP is judged at A-7c by a forged `gc_w_used > T`, under which the meta write must fail-stop (§10.5). Real GC never trips that check, so draft 7.9's B-3 form could not judge it. B-3's restart floor judges GCW-NOT-RECORDED.
    - FLIP-NO-GATE is judged again at A-7d.
    - SEG-TAIL-SHORT and LSN-CHECK-SKIP are judged at A-7d again, as at draft 7.8.
    - STEP-MUTATE-BEFORE-CHAINS is on one row.
    - Every cell that said "its own" or "the same twelve" names its mutants.
    - POISON-AFTER-UNLATCH counts at A-3c only through A-3c's own fail-stop code.
  - **PARK-DROPS-CANDIDATE at A-4e** (§15 I-CURSOR): how it fails on the found flag, which A-4e carries; its candidate half is B-1d's.
  - **Stand-ins** (§15):
    - S5 gains a rule like S1's.
    - A sixth stand-in, S6, the walk scan, is added. `t_ro_open_disk_full` runs its full scan at B-6, and `t_wal_loss_salvage`'s A-7d form names S6.
    - S3's cases gain an owner's rewrite of its provisional entry, an `on` ack, a fixture meta written with A-2's codec, and the catalog's tree list.
    - A forged input is fault injection, not a stand-in.
    - A stand-in form is a test's last form only where its row says why.
  - **Labels** (§15.1):
    - Rows that said "full" and were not now say what they run: A-8's two fixture-meta rows, `t_epoch_reserve_two_exhausters` at A-7a, `t_wal_chain_crash` and `t_ro_open_disk_full` at A-7d, and A-6b's `lsn` row.
    - The intro names the Phase B cards of the Card and Needs columns apart.
    - I-RO-OPEN's and I-WAL-COVER's Pinned-by cells gain B-6 and B-4d.
  - **Phase B count.**
    - At draft 7.9, 26 mutants were judged only on Phase B cards. 25 of them were Phase B's at draft 7.8; GCW-BOUND-SKIP was not.
    - At draft 7.10 there are 25, each Phase B's at draft 7.8.
    - §15.1's intro states the rule rather than a count.
    - Draft 7.9's "or changed" covered names only: drafts 7.9 and 7.10 change test forms and the cards that judge mutants.
  - No byte layout, persisted state, durability rule, invariant statement or SQL-visible behaviour changes. No invariant, mutant or test is added, removed or renamed.
- **Draft 7.9** (2026-11-15, card S-15-STANDINS, from PL-9-F2, `verdicts/PL-9-gate.md` check 5, and the lane map's Phase A deadlock):
  - **Stand-ins** (§15). Five test-only stand-ins (S1 flip, S2 read-back, S3 test-only caller or input, S4 sweep step, S5 second tree) and where a mutant counts.
  - **§15.1, test ownership.** One row per card that runs a test or a form of it. No Phase A row needs a Phase B card, and each Phase A row's needs lie in its card's Depends closure as the follow-on plan card sets it (A-4c on A-4b and A-7a; A-6b on A-1b; A-7c on A-6c and A-1b; A-7d on A-8 and A-1b; A-3c and A-4e lose their conflict-2 cells).
  - **Moved.** `t_root_latch_fair`'s cold variant to A-4a, re-run with a cursor at A-4e. `t_latch_scope`'s cursor fixtures and reverse variant to A-4e. I-CKPT-AT-T's splits-and-packs and async-mix test from A-7a/A-7c to A-7c, with A-7a re-running `t_epoch_reserve_two_exhausters` through `write_guard`. I-NO-INPLACE's SimEnv crash from A-7b to A-7d.
